### Limiting Disease Spreading in Human Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22191v1)
- **Authors**: Gargi Bakshi, Sujoy Bhore, Suraj Shetiya
- **Abstract**: The outbreak of a pandemic, such as COVID-19, causes major health crises worldwide. Typical measures to contain the rapid spread usually include effective vaccination and strict interventions (Nature Human Behaviour, 2021). Motivated by such circumstances, we study the problem of limiting the spread of a disease over a social network system. In their seminal work (KDD 2003), Kempe, Kleinberg, and Tardos introduced two fundamental diffusion models, the linear threshold and independent cascade, for the influence maximization problem. In this work, we adopt these models in the context of disease spreading and study effective vaccination mechanisms. Our broad goal is to limit the spread of a disease in human networks using only a limited number of vaccines. However, unlike the influence maximization problem, which typically does not require spatial awareness, disease spreading occurs in spatially structured population networks. Thus, standard Erdos-Renyi graphs do not adequately capture such networks. To address this, we study networks modeled as generalized random geometric graphs, introduced in the seminal work of Waxman (IEEE J. Sel. Areas Commun. 1988). We show that for disease spreading, the optimization function is neither submodular nor supermodular, in contrast to influence maximization, where the function is submodular. Despite this intractability, we develop novel algorithms leveraging local search and greedy techniques, which perform exceptionally well in practice. We compare them against an exact ILP-based approach to further demonstrate their robustness. Moreover, we introduce an iterative rounding mechanism for the relaxed LP formulation. Overall, our methods establish tight trade-offs between efficiency and approximation loss.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of limiting disease spread in human networks by strategically vaccinating a limited number of individuals. The authors adapt influence maximization models (Linear Threshold and Independent Cascade) to this context, focusing on spatially structured populations represented by generalized random geometric graphs.  They demonstrate that, unlike influence maximization, the optimization function in disease spreading is neither submodular nor supermodular, making standard optimization techniques less applicable. The paper introduces several algorithms, including sampling-based strategies, linear programming with relaxations (TKR and IRP), greedy approaches, and local search heuristics. The algorithms are evaluated empirically using synthetic and real-world datasets. The results show that relaxed linear programs with rounding techniques (particularly IRP) provide a good trade-off between efficiency and solution quality. The authors show that greedy and relaxed linear programs provides trade-off between efficiency and robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the core idea of adapting influence maximization techniques to disease spread isn't entirely new, the paper makes several contributions:

    *   **Focus on Spatial Structure:** Explicitly addressing spatial constraints using generalized random geometric graphs is a significant addition, as real-world disease propagation is strongly influenced by geographic proximity.
    *   **Non-Submodularity:** Identifying that the objective function is not submodular is a key negative result. It highlights the limitations of directly applying submodular optimization methods. This result also forces the authors to explore other optimization approaches, which leads to the paper's algorithmic contribution.
    *   **Algorithmic Development:**  The proposed algorithms (sampling-based, LP relaxations, greedy, local search) are well-motivated and provide a comprehensive suite of tools for the problem. The comparison between these approaches is insightful. The iterative rounding procedure is particularly interesting.
    *   **Real-world datasets (New York and Texas datasets):** The paper uses real-world population data, increasing the relevance and applicability of its findings.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant and timely, especially in the wake of the COVID-19 pandemic. The paper provides valuable insights into how to strategically allocate limited vaccination resources in spatially structured populations. It bridges the gap between influence maximization in social networks and disease spreading in real-world settings. The study of the tradeoff between vaccination levels and outcome is interesting.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The problem is well-defined, and the motivations are clearly articulated.
    *   **Comprehensive Algorithmic Suite:** The paper presents a variety of algorithms, allowing for a range of trade-offs between computational cost and solution quality.
    *   **Thorough Empirical Evaluation:** The experimental results are extensive, covering synthetic and real-world datasets. The comparison across different algorithms provides a strong basis for practical recommendations.
    *   **Well-written and Organized:** The paper is generally well-structured and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides negative results regarding submodularity, it lacks theoretical guarantees on the approximation performance of the proposed algorithms. Proving (or disproving) approximation bounds for the greedy or LP relaxation approaches would significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   **Simple Disease Models:**  The reliance on basic Linear Threshold and Independent Cascade models, while providing a tractable starting point, may oversimplify real-world disease dynamics. More complex epidemiological models (e.g., SIR, SEIR) could be considered in future work. While the work does incorporate spatiality, other network aspects like edge weight heterogeneity and dynamics could be further investigated.
    *   **Scalability:** The empirical evaluation covers networks of moderate size (up to 512 nodes). It's unclear how well the algorithms would scale to much larger networks, which are more representative of real-world populations.
    *   **Real-world validation:** While the use of real population data increases the relevance of the work, validation against real epidemiological data on vaccination strategies and disease outcomes would significantly boost the paper's impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in network science, epidemiology, and public health. It can stimulate further work on:

    *   Developing algorithms with provable approximation guarantees for disease spreading in spatial networks.
    *   Exploring more realistic disease models within the framework of influence maximization.
    *   Investigating the scalability of the proposed algorithms to very large networks.
    *   Validating the results against real-world epidemiological data.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field. The use of spatial networks is a valuable consideration and addition compared to previous work. The algorithmic development is comprehensive and the negative results on submodularity motivates the development of novel approaches. I have lowered the score for a few main reasons. There is limited theoretical analysis in terms of performance guarantees, and the models used remain simplistic. The lack of large-scale evaluation and a real-world dataset hold it back. Thus a score of 7 indicates a solid piece of work, but with areas for further refinement.

- **Classification**: cs.SI
- **Score**: 7/10

### ORIGEN: Zero-Shot 3D Orientation Grounding in Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22194v1)
- **Authors**: Yunhong Min, Daehyeon Choi, Kyeongmin Yeo, Jihyun Lee, Minhyuk Sung
- **Abstract**: We introduce ORIGEN, the first zero-shot method for 3D orientation grounding in text-to-image generation across multiple objects and diverse categories. While previous work on spatial grounding in image generation has mainly focused on 2D positioning, it lacks control over 3D orientation. To address this, we propose a reward-guided sampling approach using a pretrained discriminative model for 3D orientation estimation and a one-step text-to-image generative flow model. While gradient-ascent-based optimization is a natural choice for reward-based guidance, it struggles to maintain image realism. Instead, we adopt a sampling-based approach using Langevin dynamics, which extends gradient ascent by simply injecting random noise--requiring just a single additional line of code. Additionally, we introduce adaptive time rescaling based on the reward function to accelerate convergence. Our experiments show that ORIGEN outperforms both training-based and test-time guidance methods across quantitative metrics and user studies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ORIGEN, a novel zero-shot method for 3D orientation grounding in text-to-image generation. Unlike previous work that primarily focuses on 2D spatial control, ORIGEN enables control over the 3D orientation of multiple objects in an image, even across diverse categories.  The method uses a reward-guided sampling approach based on Langevin dynamics, which balances reward maximization (derived from a pre-trained 3D orientation estimation model) with adherence to the prior latent distribution of a generative model. A reward-adaptive time rescaling technique is also introduced to accelerate convergence. The authors create a new benchmark based on MS-COCO to quantitatively evaluate their method and demonstrate that ORIGEN outperforms existing orientation-conditioned and text-to-image generative models. User studies also validate the effectiveness of ORIGEN.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a genuine step forward in text-to-image generation by explicitly addressing 3D orientation control.  Prior work has been limited to 2D positioning or single-object orientation using synthetic training data. ORIGEN's zero-shot approach and its applicability to multiple objects in real-world images are novel and significant. The integration of Langevin dynamics for reward-guided sampling, and the reward-adaptive time rescaling contributes to a more robust and efficient method. However, the core idea of using reward-guided sampling/optimization with a pretrained model has been explored in other domains, thus the novelty here is in the application and refinement of those ideas specifically for 3D orientation grounding.
*   **Significance:**  The ability to control the 3D orientation of objects in generated images is a valuable contribution to the field. It opens up possibilities for more precise and controllable image generation, with potential applications in areas like content creation, robotics, and virtual reality. The creation of a new benchmark dataset provides a valuable resource for future research in this area. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of ORIGEN over existing methods, both quantitatively and qualitatively. The user study provides further evidence of ORIGEN's effectiveness.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Novel approach to 3D orientation grounding in text-to-image generation.
    *   Zero-shot method requiring no specialized training data.
    *   Effective reward-guided sampling using Langevin dynamics.
    *   Reward-adaptive time rescaling for accelerated convergence.
    *   Applicability to multiple objects and diverse categories.
    *   Creation of a new benchmark dataset for evaluation.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation and user study.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on OrientAnything [67]. The performance of ORIGEN is directly dependent on the accuracy and robustness of the underlying 3D orientation estimation model. Although the authors use a state-of-the-art estimator, limitations in the estimator will propagate to ORIGEN.
    *   Computational cost. Reward-guided sampling inherently involves iterative optimization steps, which can be computationally expensive compared to direct image generation. While reward-adaptive time rescaling helps accelerate convergence, the overall cost may still be a limitation for some applications.
    *   While the user study exists, additional analysis of user behaviors and feedback might enhance the paper’s claims around preference and usability.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of research in controllable image generation. It provides a solid foundation for future work on 3D spatial grounding and other forms of fine-grained control.  The proposed method and the benchmark dataset are likely to be adopted by other researchers in the field. The clarity of the paper and the comprehensive experimental results will further contribute to its impact.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** ORIGEN represents a significant advance in text-to-image generation, introducing a novel and effective approach to 3D orientation grounding. The paper is well-written, technically sound, and supported by comprehensive experimental results. While the reliance on a pre-trained estimator and the computational cost are limitations, the overall contribution is substantial and has the potential to significantly influence the field. A higher score would require further innovation on top of the current framework and further reduction to computational costs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### EdgeInfinite: A Memory-Efficient Infinite-Context Transformer for Edge Devices
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22196v1)
- **Authors**: Jiyu Chen, Shuang Peng, Daxiong Luo, Fan Yang, Renshou Wu, Fangyuan Li, Xiaoxin Chen
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based large language models (LLMs) encounter challenges in processing long sequences on edge devices due to the quadratic complexity of attention mechanisms and growing memory demands from Key-Value (KV) cache. Existing KV cache optimizations struggle with irreversible token eviction in long-output tasks, while alternative sequence modeling architectures prove costly to adopt within established Transformer infrastructure. We present EdgeInfinite, a memory-efficient solution for infinite contexts that integrates compressed memory into Transformer-based LLMs through a trainable memory-gating module. This approach maintains full compatibility with standard Transformer architectures, requiring fine-tuning only a small part of parameters, and enables selective activation of the memory-gating module for long and short context task routing. The experimental result shows that EdgeInfinite achieves comparable performance to baseline Transformer-based LLM on long context benchmarks while optimizing memory consumption and time to first token.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EdgeInfinite: A Memory-Efficient Infinite-Context Transformer for Edge Devices":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EdgeInfinite, a memory-efficient approach to handling long sequences with Transformer-based large language models (LLMs) on resource-constrained edge devices. It addresses the challenges of quadratic attention complexity and growing KV cache size. EdgeInfinite integrates compressed memory with a trainable memory-gating module while maintaining compatibility with standard Transformer architectures. It selectively activates the memory-gating module for long context tasks and preserves sink and window tokens in the KV cache to retain semantic and positional information. Experimental results on the LongBench benchmark demonstrate comparable performance to baseline Transformer models while optimizing memory consumption and time to first token (TTFT).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques for efficient long-context processing on edge devices. While individual components like memory compression and gating mechanisms exist, the authors have seemingly integrated them in a way tailored for the edge environment. The selective activation of the memory gating module depending on input length is also a practical and useful idea.

*   **Significance:** Edge deployment of LLMs is an important and growing area. The paper's focus on reducing memory consumption and TTFT directly addresses the challenges in making these models usable on devices like smartphones. A key claim of the paper is that the method retains the performance on short-text tasks without re-training, which adds to the feasibility of adoption.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practicality:** The proposed method appears to be designed with real-world edge deployment in mind. The integration with existing Transformer architectures is a significant advantage compared to methods that require entirely new model structures. The focus on both memory consumption and TTFT are well justified.
    *   **Compatibility:**  The authors maintain that EdgeInfinite is compatible with existing transformer architectures requiring only fine-tuning on the memory-gating module, which they claim involves fine-tuning only 0.15% of the parameters. This greatly reduces the cost and time commitment for training.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The use of LongBench for evaluation is appropriate for assessing long-context capabilities. The paper includes comparison with state-of-the-art memory optimization techniques like SnapKV and PyramidKV.
    *   **Ablation Studies**: The ablation studies related to sink and window tokens is well designed to explore the impact of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While LongBench is a good start, the paper could benefit from evaluation on a wider range of tasks and datasets, especially those relevant to typical edge-device applications (e.g., voice assistants, mobile AI). More specifically, more detailed ablations on the relative performance benefits on certain tasks, and why, is needed.
    *   **Implementation Details:**  The details of the memory compression are rather sparse. It should be better elaborated on how the compression is performed, and how the level of compression can be dynamically adjusted to cater to different requirements.
    *   **Limited analysis of trade-offs**: While claiming to maintain comparable performance while improving time and memory usage, a more thorough exploration of the trade-offs is needed.
    *   **Reproducibility Considerations:** The paper states the code will be released. Until then, it is difficult to fully assess the implementation details and fully verify the results.

*   **Potential Impact:** EdgeInfinite has the potential to make LLMs more accessible on edge devices, enabling a wider range of applications. The fact that it is easily integrable into already-trained transformers is another aspect that boosts the method's chance of wide-spread adoption.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a practical and potentially impactful solution for running LLMs on edge devices. Its strengths lie in its compatibility with existing architectures, focus on key performance metrics (memory and TTFT), and empirical validation. However, the limited scope of evaluation, sparse implementation details, and lack of thorough trade-off analysis holds it back. While the concept isn't fundamentally groundbreaking, the system level integration of these techniques makes the paper valuable. If the claims about retaining short-text performance and requiring minimal retraining prove solid in practice, the method is more easily adopted. A more rigorous evaluation of specific edge-device tasks is needed to push this method from interesting to truly impactful.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enhance Generation Quality of Flow Matching V2A Model via Multi-Step CoT-Like Guidance and Combined Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22200v1)
- **Authors**: Haomin Zhang, Sizhe Shan, Haoyu Wang, Zihao Chen, Xiulong Liu, Chaofan Ding, Xinhan Di
- **Abstract**: Creating high-quality sound effects from videos and text prompts requires precise alignment between visual and audio domains, both semantically and temporally, along with step-by-step guidance for professional audio generation. However, current state-of-the-art video-guided audio generation models often fall short of producing high-quality audio for both general and specialized use cases. To address this challenge, we introduce a multi-stage, multi-modal, end-to-end generative framework with Chain-of-Thought-like (CoT-like) guidance learning, termed Chain-of-Perform (CoP). First, we employ a transformer-based network architecture designed to achieve CoP guidance, enabling the generation of both general and professional audio. Second, we implement a multi-stage training framework that follows step-by-step guidance to ensure the generation of high-quality sound effects. Third, we develop a CoP multi-modal dataset, guided by video, to support step-by-step sound effects generation. Evaluation results highlight the advantages of the proposed multi-stage CoP generative framework compared to the state-of-the-art models on a variety of datasets, with FAD 0.79 to 0.74 (+6.33%), CLIP 16.12 to 17.70 (+9.80%) on VGGSound, SI-SDR 1.98dB to 3.35dB (+69.19%), MOS 2.94 to 3.49(+18.71%) on PianoYT-2h, and SI-SDR 2.22dB to 3.21dB (+44.59%), MOS 3.07 to 3.42 (+11.40%) on Piano-10h.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a multi-stage generative framework for video-to-audio (V2A) generation, called Chain-of-Perform (CoP), designed to improve the quality and alignment of generated audio, particularly in professional audio scenarios like piano performances. The framework employs a transformer-based network and CoT-like guidance learning. A multi-stage training process involves: 1) supervised training on common audio datasets (VGGSound), 2) multi-step CoP guidance training for professional audio, and 3) combined preference optimization using contrastive learning and direct preference optimization (DPO).  The paper also introduces a new multi-modal dataset (Piano-10h) to support the training of professional audio generation, specifically for piano.  The results indicate improvements in various audio quality metrics (FAD, CLIP, SI-SDR, MOS) compared to state-of-the-art models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of several components rather than a single breakthrough innovation.
    *   **CoT-like Guidance for V2A:** While Chain-of-Thought reasoning is popular in LLMs, its adaptation and application to V2A, especially with the created CoP dataset, is a valuable contribution. The concept of guiding the audio generation process step-by-step based on visual cues is potentially impactful.
    *   **Multi-Stage Training:** Multi-stage training is a common technique, but its specific implementation tailored to address the challenges of V2A (general and professional audio) adds value.
    *   **Combined Preference Optimization:** Leveraging both contrastive learning and DPO refines the model based on various style preferences in music and reduces mismatches between audio and video.
    *   **Piano-10h Dataset:** The new dataset is a significant contribution. The availability of a high-quality, multi-view dataset for video-to-piano audio generation is crucial for future research in this area.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a relevant problem – improving the quality and alignment of audio generated from videos, especially in professional contexts. The performance gains reported over existing methods (in terms of FAD, CLIP, SI-SDR, and MOS) suggest the proposed approach is effective. The Piano-10h dataset offers a valuable resource for future work.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-structured approach with multiple stages and components.
    *   Demonstrated improvements across multiple datasets and metrics.
    *   Introduction of a valuable new dataset.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental Novelty: While the combination of techniques is effective, the individual components aren't radically new.
    *   Limited Ablation Study: Deeper ablations could further clarify the contribution of individual components (e.g., comparing with just CoT or just DPO).
    *   Complexity: The multi-stage approach adds complexity, which might make the method harder to adopt and reproduce.
    *   Limited scope of evaluation: The evaluation is primarily focused on objective metrics and MOS. A more comprehensive subjective evaluation with a larger user base is recommended to fully assess the perceived quality of the generated audio.
    *   Lack of detail: More detail on how CoP is implemented would further enhance the paper. How is the knowledge explicitly represented and passed?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in video-to-audio generation, particularly in the development of methods that can handle professional audio scenarios. The Piano-10h dataset could become a benchmark for evaluating future models. The adaptation of CoT-like guidance in V2A also opens up new avenues for research.
*   **Impact:** The techniques in this paper are highly relevant and will contribute to advancements in V2A research, especially since it provides a pathway toward using more professional audio samples within such tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of V2A generation through a well-engineered multi-stage framework that combines existing techniques in a novel and effective manner. The introduction of the Piano-10h dataset is a significant asset. However, the incremental nature of the novelty and the limitations in the ablation studies and evaluation prevent a higher score. This framework makes significant steps in achieving quality audio in video to audio translation.

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeepSound-V1: Start to Think Step-by-Step in the Audio Generation from Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22208v1)
- **Authors**: Yunming Liang, Zihao Chen, Chaofan Ding, Xinhan Di
- **Abstract**: Currently, high-quality, synchronized audio is synthesized from video and optional text inputs using various multi-modal joint learning frameworks. However, the precise alignment between the visual and generated audio domains remains far from satisfactory. One key factor is the lack of sufficient temporal and semantic alignment annotations in open-source video-audio and text-audio benchmarks. Therefore, we propose a framework for audio generation from videos, leveraging the internal chain-of-thought (CoT) of a multi-modal large language model (MLLM) to enable step-by-step reasoning without requiring additional annotations. Additionally, a corresponding multi-modal reasoning dataset is constructed to facilitate the learning of initial reasoning in audio generation. In the experiments, we demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework in reducing misalignment (voice-over) in generated audio and achieving competitive performance compared to various state-of-the-art models. The evaluation results show that the proposed method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches across multiple metrics. Specifically, the F DP aSST indicator is reduced by up to 10.07%, the F DP AN N s indicator by up to 11.62%, and the F DV GG indicator by up to 38.61%. Furthermore, the IS indicator improves by up to 4.95%, the IB-score indicator increases by up to 6.39%, and the DeSync indicator is reduced by up to 0.89%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, "DeepSound-V1: Start to Think Step-by-Step in the Audio Generation from Videos":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepSound-V1, a novel framework for generating audio from videos.  It addresses the challenge of generating synchronized, high-quality audio, particularly in the presence of unwanted voice-over, by leveraging the chain-of-thought (CoT) capabilities of a multi-modal large language model (MLLM). The framework employs a step-by-step reasoning process, guiding the MLLM through generating coarse audio, identifying voice-over segments, removing them, and then determining if the resulting audio is silent. A custom multi-modal reasoning dataset is also created to train the MLLM for this specific task. The paper demonstrates that DeepSound-V1 outperforms existing state-of-the-art models in various metrics, including reducing misalignment and voice-over artifacts in generated audio. The work has three modules: Audio generation, Reasoning (MLLM with CoT), and Audio editing modules.  The audio editing module uses BS-Roformer to remove the voiceover. After the voiceover is removed, there are several strategies to deal with the resulting audio if silent.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper has some interesting novel elements but also borrows heavily from existing ideas.

*   **CoT for V2A:** The use of Chain-of-Thought reasoning in the context of Video-to-Audio generation is relatively novel.  While CoT has been explored in various vision-language tasks, its explicit application to address the specific problem of voice-over removal in V2A is a fresh approach.
*   **Multi-Modal Reasoning Dataset:** Constructing a dataset specifically designed for training the MLLM's reasoning capabilities in the audio generation context is a valuable contribution.  This dataset helps the model learn to identify and reason about voice-over elements.
*   **Framework Integration:** The integration of different modules (V2A, MLLM, Audio Editing) into a cohesive step-by-step framework is well-structured and contributes to the overall novelty.

However, BS-Roformer, V2A frameworks, and MLLMs already exist.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a contribution to the V2A synthesis. The experiments showcase improved performance on various metrics. The framework is clearly articulated and relatively easy to reproduce. The dataset created is a valuable asset. The framework can lead to more realistic and engaging audio-visual experiences. A notable point of this work is the MLLM reasoning of cross-modal data to help alleviate issues with voice-over and temporal inconsistencies in the generated video.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The framework heavily relies on existing V2A models (MMAudio, Yingsound, and FoleyCrafter), and the BS-Roformer. The improvement comes from adding MLLM reasoning, so a significant portion of the performance is dependent on the performance of the underlying base models.
*   **Limited Ablation:** While some ablation studies are performed, a more in-depth analysis of each module's contribution would strengthen the paper.  For instance, isolating the effect of the CoT reasoning itself, independent of the custom dataset, would be helpful.
*   **Dataset Scale:** While the custom dataset is useful, 18k samples might be considered relatively small compared to the vast datasets used in pre-training large language models. A larger, more diverse dataset could lead to further improvements.
*   **Subjective Evaluation:** While objective metrics are reported, a subjective evaluation (e.g., human listening tests) would provide more convincing evidence of the improved audio quality and reduced voice-over artifacts.
*   **Negative Prompt Concerns:** There is a single sentence that the "negative prompt effects the quality of generated audio." The negative prompt of "human voice" should likely help remove human voices or the voice-over, so this sentence is confusing.
*   **Silently Removed Issues:** There were also several strategies for handling silent segments after the voiceover removal, but they aren't very well justified and feel ad-hoc.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence the field by:

*   Encouraging further exploration of CoT reasoning in V2A synthesis.
*   Inspiring the creation of more targeted and specialized datasets for training MLLMs in multimodal generation tasks.
*   Providing a framework for addressing specific challenges, such as voice-over removal, in audio-visual content generation.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel application of CoT reasoning to address a practical problem in V2A synthesis. While it builds upon existing models and frameworks, the integration and the custom dataset contribute to the novelty. The experimental results demonstrate promising improvements. The paper is clearly written and provides a good foundation for future research. The score reflects this positive aspect.

However, there are several weaknesses with BS-Roformer and other existing models used, limited ablation experiments, small size of the custom dataset, the lack of a solid ablation experiments, the odd negative prompt concern, and potential ad-hoc voiceover removal strategies.

Given these limitations, the paper doesn't reach a higher score, but it has the potential to make a meaningful contribution to the field and inspires further investigation of these techniques.

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### Follow Your Motion: A Generic Temporal Consistency Portrait Editing Framework with Trajectory Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22225v1)
- **Authors**: Haijie Yang, Zhenyu Zhang, Hao Tang, Jianjun Qian, Jian Yang
- **Abstract**: Pre-trained conditional diffusion models have demonstrated remarkable potential in image editing. However, they often face challenges with temporal consistency, particularly in the talking head domain, where continuous changes in facial expressions intensify the level of difficulty. These issues stem from the independent editing of individual images and the inherent loss of temporal continuity during the editing process. In this paper, we introduce Follow Your Motion (FYM), a generic framework for maintaining temporal consistency in portrait editing. Specifically, given portrait images rendered by a pre-trained 3D Gaussian Splatting model, we first develop a diffusion model that intuitively and inherently learns motion trajectory changes at different scales and pixel coordinates, from the first frame to each subsequent frame. This approach ensures that temporally inconsistent edited avatars inherit the motion information from the rendered avatars. Secondly, to maintain fine-grained expression temporal consistency in talking head editing, we propose a dynamic re-weighted attention mechanism. This mechanism assigns higher weight coefficients to landmark points in space and dynamically updates these weights based on landmark loss, achieving more consistent and refined facial expressions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms existing approaches in terms of temporal consistency and can be used to optimize and compensate for temporally inconsistent outputs in a range of applications, such as text-driven editing, relighting, and various other applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Follow Your Motion: A Generic Temporal Consistency Portrait Editing Framework with Trajectory Guidance" introduces a novel framework, FYM, designed to enhance temporal consistency in portrait editing, particularly in the challenging domain of talking heads. The framework leverages a pre-trained 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) model for rendering and incorporates a diffusion model that learns motion trajectory changes at different scales and pixel coordinates. A dynamic re-weighted attention mechanism is proposed to maintain fine-grained expression consistency by assigning higher weights to landmark points and updating these weights based on landmark loss. The paper demonstrates through experiments that FYM outperforms existing approaches in temporal consistency and can optimize and compensate for inconsistencies in applications like text-driven editing and relighting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques to address the temporal consistency problem in talking-head portrait editing. While individual components like diffusion models, 3DGS, and attention mechanisms are not entirely new, the integrated framework and the specific way motion trajectories are learned and used as guidance are innovative. The dynamic re-weighting attention mechanism is another novel component tailored for fine-grained facial expression consistency. However, the idea of incorporating motion or optical flow for video editing tasks is not groundbreaking; the authors build upon this idea and tailor it for high-fidelity talking heads by incorporating 3D priors.

*   **Significance:** Temporal consistency is a significant challenge in video editing, and this paper addresses a crucial aspect of it: maintaining consistent facial expressions and motion in portrait videos. The proposed framework has the potential to improve the quality and realism of edited talking-head videos. It is shown to optimize and compensate for inconsistencies in applications like text-driven editing and relighting, increasing the usability. The experimental results suggest that the method provides better quantitative results in temporal consistency and also qualitative improvements.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is well-defined and modular, with clear explanations of each component.
    *   The use of 3DGS for initial rendering provides a strong foundation for generating high-quality portraits.
    *   The motion trajectory-guided diffusion model effectively captures and preserves temporal information.
    *   The dynamic re-weighted attention mechanism addresses the fine-grained expression consistency issue effectively.
    *   The experimental results are promising and demonstrate the superiority of FYM over existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method relies on pre-trained models (3DGS, diffusion model) and has an edit-specific training component, which can limit its flexibility.
    *   While the qualitative results are visually appealing, the quantitative metrics rely on proxy measures such as optical flow variation and CLIP scores, which do not directly assess perceptual quality. The user study helps, but more extensive user studies would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   As stated in the paper, the reliance on the initial rendering quality from the Gaussian rendering stage means that it is only able to address temporal consistency issues.
    *   Although the authors claim the method is generic, it is explicitly designed for talking heads which inherently limits its generalization ability and makes it difficult to transfer to other domains or scenarios.
    *   The ablation study could be improved by including more controlled experiments that isolate the contribution of each component.

*   **Potential Influence:** The FYM framework has the potential to influence future research in portrait video editing by providing a new approach to addressing temporal consistency issues. It is likely to spur further development in incorporating 3D priors and motion information into diffusion-based editing frameworks. Future work would ideally focus on alleviating edit-specific training components and improving the generalizability of the framework to other video editing tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of portrait video editing by introducing a novel framework that effectively addresses the challenge of temporal consistency. The combination of 3DGS, a motion trajectory-guided diffusion model, and a dynamic re-weighted attention mechanism is innovative. The results demonstrate the superiority of FYM over existing approaches, indicating the potential impact on the field. However, the method's dependence on pre-trained models, edit-specific training, reliance on Gaussian rendering, and limited generalizability to talking heads constraints its overall significance.

In summary, while FYM offers a compelling solution to a specific problem and has the potential to influence future research, the limitations prevent it from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploring Data Scaling Trends and Effects in Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22230v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Shen, Guanlin Liu, Zheng Wu, Ruofei Zhu, Qingping Yang, Chao Xin, Yu Yue, Lin Yan
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) is crucial for aligning large language models with human preferences. While recent research has focused on algorithmic improvements, the importance of prompt-data construction has been overlooked. This paper addresses this gap by exploring data-driven bottlenecks in RLHF performance scaling, particularly reward hacking and decreasing response diversity. We introduce a hybrid reward system combining reasoning task verifiers (RTV) and a generative reward model (GenRM) to mitigate reward hacking. We also propose a novel prompt-selection method, Pre-PPO, to maintain response diversity and enhance learning effectiveness. Additionally, we find that prioritizing mathematical and coding tasks early in RLHF training significantly improves performance. Experiments across two model sizes validate our methods' effectiveness and scalability. Results show that RTV is most resistant to reward hacking, followed by GenRM with ground truth, and then GenRM with SFT Best-of-N responses. Our strategies enable rapid capture of subtle task-specific distinctions, leading to substantial improvements in overall RLHF performance. This work highlights the importance of careful data construction and provides practical methods to overcome performance barriers in RLHF.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Exploring Data Scaling Trends and Effects in Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates data-driven bottlenecks in Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) that hinder performance scaling.  It focuses on two key challenges: reward hacking and the loss of response diversity. To mitigate reward hacking, the authors propose a hybrid reward system combining reasoning task verifiers (RTV) and a generative reward model (GenRM). To improve response diversity and learning effectiveness, they introduce a novel prompt selection method called Pre-PPO, which identifies inherently challenging training prompts.  They also find that prioritizing mathematical and coding tasks during the early stages of RLHF training boosts performance.  The effectiveness and scalability of the proposed methods are validated through experiments across two model sizes.  The results show that RTV exhibits the strongest resistance to reward hacking. The strategies allow the model to capture task-specific distinctions and substantially improve RLHF performance. The paper highlights the importance of careful data construction and practical methodologies to overcome performance barriers in RLHF.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Focus on Data Construction:** The paper addresses a crucial, often overlooked aspect of RLHF – the creation and structure of training data. Much of the literature focuses on algorithmic improvements, making this data-centric perspective valuable.
    *   **Practical Solutions:** The Pre-PPO prompt selection and the hybrid reward system (RTV + GenRM) are practical and implementable techniques. The early prioritization of coding/math tasks provides a concrete, actionable strategy.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The paper includes comprehensive experiments across different model sizes and evaluation datasets, providing evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed methods. The ablation studies are particularly useful for isolating the impact of each component.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the issues of reward hacking and diminishing response diversity as major hindrances to RLHF scaling, providing a strong motivation for their work.
    * **Rigorous analysis**: The paper identifies a clear correlation between high reward scores and more severe reward hacking, highlighting a significant gap in the current reward model that emphasizes the need for better evaluation metrics.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Task Types:** While the paper highlights the benefits of prioritizing mathematical and coding tasks, its exploration of data construction and scaling might be less directly applicable to more subjective tasks like creative writing or social interaction, where clear ground truths are harder to define.
    *   **Pre-PPO Scalability:** The Pre-PPO prompt selection method relies on evaluating prompts with a reward model, which adds computational overhead. The paper mentions using the smaller model for Pre-PPO, but the computational cost of this step for very large language models (VLMs) isn't fully addressed.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the gains are consistently positive, the overall performance improvements observed are moderate. This suggests that while the proposed methods are valuable, they might be best seen as components of a larger RLHF improvement strategy, rather than silver bullets.
    *   **Limited Generalization** The study acknowledges that the best RLHF performance was comparable to simply sampling five responses from the SFT model, highlighting a potential challenge in surpassing performance through RLHF and suggesting the need for further research into enhancing its effectiveness.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The focus on data construction methods for RLHF is relatively novel. The Pre-PPO prompt selection and the hybrid reward system contribute new, practical approaches to the field. The finding that early prioritization of mathematical/coding tasks boosts RLHF performance is also a novel observation.
    *   **Significance:**  The paper's findings have the potential to significantly impact RLHF practitioners. By focusing on practical data construction and training strategies, the paper provides actionable insights that can improve the performance, robustness, and scalability of RLHF systems. The identification of clear challenges and bottlenecks in RLHF scaling is valuable for guiding future research efforts.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents solid, well-supported research on a practical problem in RLHF. It’s focus on data creation rather than the RL algorithm stands out as a novel contribution. The individual pieces, e.g., reward mixing, or prioritization are all known ideas, but the combination and insights on their importance in the context of data scarcity is new. While the performance improvements are not revolutionary and the scope is limited, the paper offers valuable insights and concrete strategies for improving RLHF systems.  The limitations in task generalization also prevent it from achieving a higher score. Thus, after careful consideration of both strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Integrating LLMs in Software Engineering Education: Motivators, Demotivators, and a Roadmap Towards a Framework for Finnish Higher Education Institutes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22238v1)
- **Authors**: Maryam Khan, Muhammad Azeem Akbar, Jussi Kasurinen
- **Abstract**: The increasing adoption of Large Language Models (LLMs) in software engineering education presents both opportunities and challenges. While LLMs offer benefits such as enhanced learning experiences, automated assessments, and personalized tutoring, their integration also raises concerns about academic integrity, student over-reliance, and ethical considerations. In this study, we conducted a preliminary literature review to identify motivators and demotivators for using LLMs in software engineering education. We applied a thematic mapping process to categorize and structure these factors (motivators and demotivators), offering a comprehensive view of their impact. In total, we identified 25 motivators and 30 demotivators, which are further organized into four high-level themes. This mapping provides a structured framework for understanding the factors that influence the integration of LLMs in software engineering education, both positively and negatively. As part of a larger research project, this study serves as a feasibility assessment, laying the groundwork for future systematic literature review and empirical studies. Ultimately, this project aims to develop a framework to assist Finnish higher education institutions in effectively integrating LLMs into software engineering education while addressing potential risks and challenges.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a preliminary literature review identifying motivators and demotivators for integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into software engineering (SE) education, specifically focusing on Finnish higher education institutions.  It categorizes these factors into high-level themes using a thematic mapping process. The authors identify 25 motivators and 30 demotivators. The research serves as a feasibility study for a larger project aiming to develop a framework for effectively integrating LLMs into SE education while addressing risks. The study also proposes a research method using SLR, empirical studies like interviews, and questionnaires, ultimately aiming to establish guidelines for institutions, policymakers, students, and curriculum developers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Timely and Relevant Topic:**  The increasing use of LLMs in education is a hot topic, making this work relevant and potentially impactful.  Specifically focusing on software engineering education is a good niche.
    *   **Structured Approach:** The thematic mapping process and categorization of motivators/demotivators provide a structured overview of the potential impacts of LLMs.
    *   **Finnish Context:** Focusing on the Finnish education system is a strength, potentially leading to context-specific and useful guidelines.
    *   **Comprehensive Scope (Preliminary):** The inclusion of both motivators and demotivators provides a balanced view, rather than just focusing on the benefits of LLMs.
    *   **Clearly Defined Research Questions and Methodology**: The methodology involving the snowballing approach and empirical studies is well-structured and logical.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Preliminary Nature:** The paper is explicitly a preliminary literature review, limiting the depth and rigor of the findings. It acknowledges limitations like a lack of empirical data.
    *   **Novelty of Findings:** The specific motivators and demotivators identified are largely predictable based on existing discussions around AI in education.  The analysis, while structured, may not offer significantly new insights *at this stage*.
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The conclusions are drawn from a literature review and require empirical validation to assess their real-world applicability. The Kendall's Coefficient of Concordance addresses *interpersonal* bias between reviewers, which is good, but doesn't speak to *selection* bias present within the literature itself.
    *   **Generalizability beyond Finland:** Although the Finnish context is a strength, the specific framework for Finnish education *may* limit the applicability of the findings elsewhere.
    *   **Lack of critical analysis of prior research**: There appears to be a summary of prior research in the "Related Work" section, but little critical engagement with it.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While acknowledging that it's a preliminary study, the discussion of limitations could be more in-depth and specifically address potential biases in the literature selected.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies primarily in its potential as a starting point for more in-depth research and development of practical guidelines. It lays the groundwork for future studies by synthesizing existing perspectives and framing the key considerations for integrating LLMs.
    *   The focus on the Finnish education system offers a unique contribution by tailoring the framework to a specific context, potentially enhancing its relevance and impact within that region.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's strengths are primarily in its timely topic and structured approach to synthesizing existing literature. The weaknesses stem from its preliminary nature and lack of substantial new findings. The novelty is incremental, primarily in the application of known concepts to a specific context (Finnish SE education).  The significance is limited by the lack of empirical validation. However, it provides a useful synthesis and framework for future work.

### Agent-Centric Personalized Multiple Clustering with Multi-Modal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22241v1)
- **Authors**: Ziye Chen, Yiqun Duan, Riheng Zhu, Zhenbang Sun, Mingming Gong
- **Abstract**: Personalized multiple clustering aims to generate diverse partitions of a dataset based on different user-specific aspects, rather than a single clustering. It has recently drawn research interest for accommodating varying user preferences. Recent approaches primarily use CLIP embeddings with proxy learning to extract representations biased toward user clustering preferences. However, CLIP primarily focuses on coarse image-text alignment, lacking a deep contextual understanding of user interests. To overcome these limitations, we propose an agent-centric personalized clustering framework that leverages multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) as agents to comprehensively traverse a relational graph to search for clusters based on user interests. Due to the advanced reasoning mechanism of MLLMs, the obtained clusters align more closely with user-defined criteria than those obtained from CLIP-based representations. To reduce computational overhead, we shorten the agents' traversal path by constructing a relational graph using user-interest-biased embeddings extracted by MLLMs. A large number of weakly connected edges can be filtered out based on embedding similarity, facilitating an efficient traversal search for agents. Experimental results show that the proposed method achieves NMI scores of 0.9667 and 0.9481 on the Card Order and Card Suits benchmarks, respectively, largely improving the SOTA model by over 140%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces an agent-centric framework for personalized multiple clustering. Unlike existing methods that primarily rely on CLIP embeddings and proxy learning, this approach leverages multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) as agents to traverse a relational graph and search for clusters based on user-defined interests. To improve efficiency, the relational graph is constructed using MLLM-generated embeddings biased towards user interests, filtering out weakly connected edges. Experimental results on several benchmark datasets demonstrate significant improvements over existing methods, indicating that the proposed framework more accurately reflects user-defined criteria.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using MLLMs as agents for traversing a relational graph to perform personalized clustering is novel. Prior work in personalized multiple clustering relies on CLIP and struggles to capture complex user preferences.  The agent-centric approach allows for more dynamic and contextual reasoning.
*   **Significance:** The problem of personalized multiple clustering is practically relevant, as users often have diverse preferences and perspectives on data.  The performance gains demonstrated across multiple benchmarks highlight the potential of this approach to better serve user needs in various applications.
*   **Technical Soundness:** The paper provides a detailed explanation of the proposed framework, including the construction of the relational graph, the agent-centric traversal process, and the MLLM-based graph construction. The ablation studies offer insights into the importance of graph density and embedding selection.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical results are strong, showcasing substantial improvements over state-of-the-art methods on several well-known benchmarks. The use of NMI and RI metrics provides a clear and objective measure of clustering performance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:**  While the authors address the computational overhead by using MLLM-biased embeddings for graph construction, the use of MLLMs as agents is still inherently computationally expensive. The paper does not thoroughly explore the trade-off between accuracy and efficiency, and there are no detailed benchmarks reported on computational time or resources.
*   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Training Labels:**  The reliance on GPT-4 for generating training labels for the MLLM fine-tuning could introduce biases and limit the generalizability of the approach. The quality of the generated labels directly impacts the performance of the MLLM, which is a potential point of failure. The paper should provide a discussion on how the potential biases within GPT-4 and the label generation process may impact their findings.
*   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:**  While the paper provides quantitative results, it lacks a more detailed qualitative analysis of the clusters generated.  It would be helpful to see examples of how the MLLM agents reason about data relationships and how this reasoning leads to improved clustering outcomes. This would strengthen the argument that MLLMs are capturing the complexity of user interests.
*   **Limited Exploration of Agent Architectures:** The paper treats all agents identically other than their cluster assignments. Exploring different types of agents with more customized decision-making processes based on initial clustering may be an interesting direction to improve performance.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and promising approach to personalized multiple clustering with impressive performance, the reliance on expensive MLLM and the training data generation using another LLM raises concerns about accessibility and scalability. The current results suggest that, in some scenarios, the paper would not be feasible for many applications, and further effort should be spent on reducing the computational overhead.

### Beyond the Script: Testing LLMs for Authentic Patient Communication Styles in Healthcare
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22250v1)
- **Authors**: Anna Bodonhelyi, Christian Stegemann-Philipps, Alessandra Sonanini, Lea Herschbach, Márton Szép, Anne Herrmann-Werner, Teresa Festl-Wietek, Enkelejda Kasneci, Friederike Holderried
- **Abstract**: Effective patient communication is pivotal in healthcare, yet traditional medical training often lacks exposure to diverse, challenging interpersonal dynamics. To bridge this gap, this study proposes the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to simulate authentic patient communication styles, specifically the "accuser" and "rationalizer" personas derived from the Satir model, while also ensuring multilingual applicability to accommodate diverse cultural contexts and enhance accessibility for medical professionals. Leveraging advanced prompt engineering, including behavioral prompts, author's notes, and stubbornness mechanisms, we developed virtual patients (VPs) that embody nuanced emotional and conversational traits. Medical professionals evaluated these VPs, rating their authenticity (accuser: $3.8 \pm 1.0$; rationalizer: $3.7 \pm 0.8$ on a 5-point Likert scale (from one to five)) and correctly identifying their styles. Emotion analysis revealed distinct profiles: the accuser exhibited pain, anger, and distress, while the rationalizer displayed contemplation and calmness, aligning with predefined, detailed patient description including medical history. Sentiment scores (on a scale from zero to nine) further validated these differences in the communication styles, with the accuser adopting negative ($3.1 \pm 0.6$) and the rationalizer more neutral ($4.0 \pm 0.4$) tone. These results underscore LLMs' capability to replicate complex communication styles, offering transformative potential for medical education. This approach equips trainees to navigate challenging clinical scenarios by providing realistic, adaptable patient interactions, enhancing empathy and diagnostic acumen. Our findings advocate for AI-driven tools as scalable, cost-effective solutions to cultivate nuanced communication skills, setting a foundation for future innovations in healthcare training.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to simulate authentic patient communication styles in healthcare, specifically focusing on the "accuser" and "rationalizer" personas from the Satir model. The authors employed prompt engineering techniques (author's note, behavior instructions, stubbornness mechanism) to develop virtual patients (VPs) with nuanced emotional and conversational traits. Medical professionals evaluated these VPs, assessing their authenticity and correctly identifying their styles. Emotion analysis further confirmed the distinct emotional profiles of the VPs. The study suggests that LLMs can effectively replicate complex communication styles, offering potential for transformative medical education.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Relevant and Timely Topic:** The paper addresses a critical gap in medical education: the lack of exposure to diverse and challenging interpersonal dynamics. Using LLMs to simulate patient communication styles is a relevant application of current technology.
    * **Rigorous Methodology:** The study combines prompt engineering, user evaluation by medical professionals, and automated emotion analysis to validate the authenticity and effectiveness of the VPs. The detailed description of the prompt engineering techniques contributes to the replicability of the results.
    * **Clear Results and Insights:** The results provide evidence that medical professionals were able to identify and rate VP authenticity as generally "authentic". Furthermore, the emotion analysis corroborates the predefined emotional profiles of the VPs, strengthening the argument that LLMs can be used to model specific personalities.
    * **Emphasis on Ethical Considerations:** The authors acknowledge the limitations of the approach and emphasize the importance of balancing realism with educational value.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The study focuses only on two communication styles (accuser and rationalizer), which limits the generalizability of the findings to other patient personas.
    * **Potential for Bias:** The reliance on the Satir model, while providing a structured framework, may introduce biases in how patient communication styles are defined and implemented. The model is a specific theoretical perspective and might not universally capture all nuances of human interaction.
    * **Participant Pool:** The participant pool is primarily composed of psychotherapists. This limits the representativeness of their findings as this group may already have a unique understanding of the modelled communication styles.
   * **GPT-4 Limitations:** The paper mentions that the results were stronger when using GPT-4, implicitly acknowledging the limitations of using weaker LLMs which calls into question the affordability and accessibility of the platform in contexts with limited resouces.
    * **Limited Novelty:** While the application of LLMs in this specific context is interesting, the prompt engineering techniques employed are not particularly novel. The core concept of using LLMs for simulating conversations or role-playing is not groundbreaking.

* **Significance:** The paper highlights the potential of LLMs for personalized and adaptive medical education. The ability to simulate diverse patient communication styles could enhance empathy and diagnostic skills among medical trainees. The research advocates for the use of AI-driven tools as a scalable and cost-effective solution in healthcare training.

* **Justification for Score:** While the study has merits in terms of its methodology and relevance, its novelty is somewhat limited. It builds upon existing research on LLM-based conversational agents and prompt engineering techniques. Furthermore, the limited scope of the study and the participant pool restrict the generalizability of its findings. The findings themselves are generally positive, but do not present a major paradigm shift. A score of 6 reflects this balance of strengths and weaknesses.

### Mono2Stereo: A Benchmark and Empirical Study for Stereo Conversion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22262v1)
- **Authors**: Songsong Yu, Yuxin Chen, Zhongang Qi, Zeke Xie, Yifan Wang, Lijun Wang, Ying Shan, Huchuan Lu
- **Abstract**: With the rapid proliferation of 3D devices and the shortage of 3D content, stereo conversion is attracting increasing attention. Recent works introduce pretrained Diffusion Models (DMs) into this task. However, due to the scarcity of large-scale training data and comprehensive benchmarks, the optimal methodologies for employing DMs in stereo conversion and the accurate evaluation of stereo effects remain largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce the Mono2Stereo dataset, providing high-quality training data and benchmark to support in-depth exploration of stereo conversion. With this dataset, we conduct an empirical study that yields two primary findings. 1) The differences between the left and right views are subtle, yet existing metrics consider overall pixels, failing to concentrate on regions critical to stereo effects. 2) Mainstream methods adopt either one-stage left-to-right generation or warp-and-inpaint pipeline, facing challenges of degraded stereo effect and image distortion respectively. Based on these findings, we introduce a new evaluation metric, Stereo Intersection-over-Union, which prioritizes disparity and achieves a high correlation with human judgments on stereo effect. Moreover, we propose a strong baseline model, harmonizing the stereo effect and image quality simultaneously, and notably surpassing current mainstream methods. Our code and data will be open-sourced to promote further research in stereo conversion. Our models are available at mono2stereo-bench.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Mono2Stereo: A Benchmark and Empirical Study for Stereo Conversion" addresses the problem of converting monocular images to stereoscopic (3D) images. It introduces a new large-scale dataset called Mono2Stereo, containing over 2.4 million stereo image pairs across diverse scenes. Based on empirical studies using this dataset, the authors identify limitations in existing evaluation metrics (RMSE, SSIM, PSNR) for accurately assessing stereo effects, and propose a new metric called Stereo Intersection-over-Union (SIoU) that prioritizes disparity consistency and edge structure. They also develop a strong baseline model for stereo conversion, utilizing a dual-conditional diffusion model and an Edge Consistency loss, which outperforms existing methods in both image quality and stereo effect.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper makes several contributions, each with varying degrees of novelty:
    * **Mono2Stereo Dataset:**  The creation of a large-scale, diverse stereo image dataset is a significant contribution, addressing a key limitation in the field.  While large datasets are increasingly common, the specific nature of *paired* stereo images is still valuable.
    * **Stereo Intersection-over-Union (SIoU):** The SIoU metric is a novel attempt to address the shortcomings of traditional metrics for stereo conversion.  The core idea of focusing on disparity and edge consistency is well-motivated.  The extent to which SIoU correlates with human perception, rigorously proven, needs further scrutiny beyond the paper's own experiments.
    * **Dual-Conditional Diffusion Model:**  The use of dual-conditioning in a diffusion model is not entirely new, as other works have explored similar ideas.  However, the specific application to stereo conversion, the careful selection of geometric and viewpoint conditions, and the introduction of Edge Consistency loss can be considered novel.
    * **Edge Consistency Loss:** The idea to minimize the difference between edges is conceptually simple, its application to the latent space in this setup is new.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the following factors:
    * **Addressing a Gap:**  Stereo conversion is becoming increasingly relevant with the rise of AR/VR, but the lack of suitable data and evaluation metrics has hindered progress. The paper directly addresses these limitations.
    * **Empirical Insights:** The paper's empirical study provides valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of different approaches to stereo conversion, informing future research directions.
    * **Strong Baseline:**  The proposed baseline model sets a new performance standard for stereo conversion, demonstrating the effectiveness of the dual-conditioning approach and Edge Consistency loss.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Large-scale Dataset:** The Mono2Stereo dataset is a valuable resource for the community.
    * **Well-Motivated Metric:** SIoU addresses a clear limitation of existing metrics and is based on reasonable assumptions about stereo perception.
    * **Improved Performance:** The proposed baseline model achieves state-of-the-art performance on both in-domain and out-of-domain datasets.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methods and experimental results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Subjectivity of Stereo Quality:** Assessing stereo quality is inherently subjective. While human evaluation is included, the number of annotators is relatively small, and the potential for bias needs to be considered. The paper needs to demonstrate with further rigorous study the higher correlation of SIoU with human judgements by minimizing confounding factors like number of annotators or inherent biases.
    * **Limited Comparison:** While the paper compares against several existing methods, a more comprehensive comparison to a wider range of state-of-the-art techniques could strengthen the evaluation. It's also unclear if all compared methods were optimized under similar training settings.
    * **Dataset Bias:** While the dataset is large, it's still possible that it contains biases that could affect the generalizability of the results. Further analysis of dataset characteristics could be beneficial.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** The technical contributions are incremental, building on existing diffusion model architectures and loss functions. The primary innovation is in their application to stereo conversion and in the proposed evaluation metric.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.

*Reasoning:*  The paper makes significant and practically valuable contributions to the field of stereo conversion by providing a large dataset, a more appropriate evaluation metric, and an improved baseline model. These contributions help address existing challenges and advance the state-of-the-art in stereo conversion. However, the novelty of the technical components (dual-conditioning and Edge Consistency loss) is somewhat incremental, and the paper could benefit from a more comprehensive evaluation and more scrutiny in the subjective qualities. The SIoU's higher correlation with human perception of stereo, and its validity needs more rigorously tested, beyond the paper's own experiments.
