### Negation: A Pink Elephant in the Large Language Models' Room?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22395v1)
- **Authors**: Tereza Vrabcová, Marek Kadlčík, Petr Sojka, Michal Štefánik, Michal Spiegel
- **Abstract**: Negations are key to determining sentence meaning, making them essential for logical reasoning. Despite their importance, negations pose a substantial challenge for large language models (LLMs) and remain underexplored. We construct two multilingual natural language inference (NLI) datasets with \textit{paired} examples differing in negation. We investigate how model size and language impact its ability to handle negation correctly by evaluating popular LLMs. Contrary to previous work, we show that increasing the model size consistently improves the models' ability to handle negations. Furthermore, we find that both the models' reasoning accuracy and robustness to negation are language-dependent and that the length and explicitness of the premise have a greater impact on robustness than language. Our datasets can facilitate further research and improvements of language model reasoning in multilingual settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of large language models (LLMs) to reason under negation, a known challenge for these models. The authors create two multilingual natural language inference (NLI) datasets with paired examples differing primarily in negation. They evaluate several popular LLMs with varying sizes and architectures across four languages (English, Czech, German, and Ukrainian). The key findings are that (1) increasing model size generally improves the handling of negation, (2) performance is language-dependent, and (3) the explicitness and length of the premise have a larger impact on robustness than the language itself. Contrary to previous findings, this research supports the idea that the performance of LLMs regarding negation improves as model size increases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Multilingual Approach:** The paper's examination of LLMs across multiple languages with varying linguistic properties (word order flexibility, morphology, etc.) is a significant strength.  Most existing research focuses primarily on English, limiting the generalizability of findings.

    *   **Dataset Creation:**  The creation and release of new NLI datasets specifically designed to test negation reasoning in multiple languages is a valuable contribution. These resources can be used by other researchers to further explore this problem. While extending existing datasets like FEVER and SNLI, the manual review for translation accuracy and generated hypotheses is an essential component which bolsters the validity of their dataset.

    *   **Contradicts Existing Literature:** The result that LLMs are in fact impacted positively as size increases directly contradicts previous findings, indicating novelty in their dataset or experimental methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Selection:** While the paper evaluates several LLMs, the selection could be broader, incorporating models from different architectural families or those specifically designed for multilingual reasoning. A comparative performance analysis on a single dataset using a diverse range of LLMs could be a valuable addition to the existing literature.

    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The datasets built upon existing NLI datasets (FEVER and SNLI), this introduces potential biases and doesn't explicitly explore more complex forms of negation. Sub-clausal negation is not tackled. It is still hard to assess the dataset's real-world applicability and challenge level. The impact of any dataset-specific quirks should be better addressed and compared against previous approaches.

    *   **Analysis Depth:** Some conclusions, particularly the language dependence, are not strongly supported by statistical significance across all the model families. While there's a general trend, the lack of statistical significance in certain cases weakens the conclusions and requires further exploration.

    *   **Novelty Concerns:** The key finding around model size scaling has been hinted at in recent prior art. While this paper provides evidence directly contradicting previous works, the increase in size resulting in increased accuracy is not an unexpected result given recent trends in the field.

    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The paper identifies that models may have inadvertently been trained on test data, it fails to explore how this impacted performance.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes a valuable multilingual dataset for evaluating negation understanding in LLMs, which can spur further research.
    *   The direct contradiction of existing findings about LLMs' ability to handle negation has novel components in their dataset/methodology.
    *   However, some limitations in dataset scope and analysis depth reduce the overall impact.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The dataset could be widely adopted by researchers interested in improving negation handling in LLMs.
    *   The counter-intuitive findings related to model size and dataset impacts will likely stimulate further research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a tangible resource (the multilingual dataset) and contributes to the understanding of how LLMs reason with negation, particularly regarding scaling trends, and in a multilingual context. While its scope of negation types is relatively narrow, and some of its conclusions require more solid statistical support, the finding that increased size improves negation performance directly contradicts existing literature and opens the door for novel architectural or pre-training paradigms that leverage this scale. The overall impact is somewhat diminished by the limitations outlined.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### GAITGen: Disentangled Motion-Pathology Impaired Gait Generative Model -- Bringing Motion Generation to the Clinical Domain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22397v1)
- **Authors**: Vida Adeli, Soroush Mehraban, Majid Mirmehdi, Alan Whone, Benjamin Filtjens, Amirhossein Dadashzadeh, Alfonso Fasano, Andrea Iaboni Babak Taati
- **Abstract**: Gait analysis is crucial for the diagnosis and monitoring of movement disorders like Parkinson's Disease. While computer vision models have shown potential for objectively evaluating parkinsonian gait, their effectiveness is limited by scarce clinical datasets and the challenge of collecting large and well-labelled data, impacting model accuracy and risk of bias. To address these gaps, we propose GAITGen, a novel framework that generates realistic gait sequences conditioned on specified pathology severity levels. GAITGen employs a Conditional Residual Vector Quantized Variational Autoencoder to learn disentangled representations of motion dynamics and pathology-specific factors, coupled with Mask and Residual Transformers for conditioned sequence generation. GAITGen generates realistic, diverse gait sequences across severity levels, enriching datasets and enabling large-scale model training in parkinsonian gait analysis. Experiments on our new PD-GaM (real) dataset demonstrate that GAITGen outperforms adapted state-of-the-art models in both reconstruction fidelity and generation quality, accurately capturing critical pathology-specific gait features. A clinical user study confirms the realism and clinical relevance of our generated sequences. Moreover, incorporating GAITGen-generated data into downstream tasks improves parkinsonian gait severity estimation, highlighting its potential for advancing clinical gait analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GAITGen paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GAITGen, a novel generative model for creating realistic gait sequences conditioned on Parkinson's Disease (PD) pathology severity.  GAITGen uses a Conditional Residual Vector Quantized Variational Autoencoder (RVQ-VAE) to disentangle motion dynamics and pathology-specific factors.  This disentanglement is then combined with Mask and Residual Transformers for conditioned sequence generation.  The authors also contribute a new dataset, PD-GaM, a 3D mesh dataset of gait sequences with UPDRS-gait scores. The paper demonstrates that GAITGen outperforms existing models in reconstruction fidelity and generation quality, capturing essential pathology-specific gait features. A clinical user study confirms the realism and clinical relevance of the generated sequences.  Furthermore, using GAITGen-generated data to augment downstream tasks improves parkinsonian gait severity estimation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.
    *   The application of a generative model specifically designed for pathology-conditioned gait generation is new.  Previous work has focused on text-to-motion or style transfer but not on generating clinically relevant movements affected by disease.
    *   The RVQ-VAE architecture with specific mechanisms to enforce disentanglement between motion and pathology is a significant contribution.
    *   The PD-GaM dataset fills a gap in publicly available, ethically sourced gait datasets with UPDRS-gait scores.
    *   The introduction of clinically relevant evaluation metrics tailored for the pathology-conditioned gait generation task is a valuable contribution as it establishes a foundation for assessing generative models in clinical motion analysis.
    * Mix-and-Match augmentation strategy enabled by the disentangled latent space for severe gait impairment data generation, is a significant contribution.

* **Significance:**
    *   Addressing the data scarcity problem in medical imaging, particularly for movement disorders like PD, is highly significant.  The ability to generate realistic and diverse gait sequences allows for more robust machine learning model training.
    *   The clinical user study provides strong evidence of the model's realism and clinical relevance, which is crucial for acceptance and application in the medical domain.
    *   The improvement in downstream task performance (parkinsonian gait severity estimation) demonstrates the practical utility of the generated data.
    *   The paper provides a clear pathway for improving diagnostic tools and understanding of movement disorders.
    * It can facilitate better evaluation of gait abnormalities, enhance early disease diagnosis, personalized therapy, and monitoring.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper's architecture and methodology are well described and justified.
    * The experimental results are comprehensive, including comparisons to baselines, ablation studies, and a clinical user study.
    *  The PD-GaM dataset is a valuable resource for the community.
    * The clinical user study is convincing and strengthens the paper's claims.
    *  Ablation studies and discussion is detailed.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * While the paper highlights the need for diversity in synthetic data, the methods for ensuring diversity could be elaborated upon further. Although Gumbel-Softmax and Top-K Masking is discussed, it lacks sufficient explanation.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of the limitations of the generated data. The generated samples could be influenced or constrained by the biases present in the training data.
    * A potential weakness lies in the relatively small size of the new dataset.

* **Potential Influence:**
    * GAITGen has the potential to significantly impact the field of computer vision for medical applications, specifically in movement disorder analysis.
    *   The PD-GaM dataset can serve as a benchmark for future research in this area.
    *   The proposed framework and evaluation metrics can be adapted for other movement disorders and clinical applications.

* **Conclusion:** The GAITGen paper represents a substantial advance in generative models for clinical motion analysis. The disentangled architecture, new dataset, clinical validation, and improved downstream task performance are significant contributions. While there are areas for improvement, the paper addresses a crucial problem (data scarcity) and provides a promising solution with clear clinical relevance.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:** The score reflects the paper's strong novelty and significance, particularly in addressing data scarcity in a clinically relevant application. While there are limitations related to diversity and scope, the overall contribution is substantial and has the potential to influence future research in this area. The thorough evaluation, including the clinical user study, adds significant weight to the paper's claims. A score of 8.5 reflects the paper's significant contribution, while acknowledging that further research can build upon these findings to address the identified limitations and expand the model's capabilities.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Generative Reliability-Based Design Optimization Using In-Context Learning Capabilities of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22401v1)
- **Authors**: Zhonglin Jiang, Qian Tang, Zequn Wang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable in-context learning capabilities, enabling flexible utilization of limited historical information to play pivotal roles in reasoning, problem-solving, and complex pattern recognition tasks. Inspired by the successful applications of LLMs in multiple domains, this paper proposes a generative design method by leveraging the in-context learning capabilities of LLMs with the iterative search mechanisms of metaheuristic algorithms for solving reliability-based design optimization problems. In detail, reliability analysis is performed by engaging the LLMs and Kriging surrogate modeling to overcome the computational burden. By dynamically providing critical information of design points to the LLMs with prompt engineering, the method enables rapid generation of high-quality design alternatives that satisfy reliability constraints while achieving performance optimization. With the Deepseek-V3 model, three case studies are used to demonstrated the performance of the proposed approach. Experimental results indicate that the proposed LLM-RBDO method successfully identifies feasible solutions that meet reliability constraints while achieving a comparable convergence rate compared to traditional genetic algorithms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel generative reliability-based design optimization (RBDO) framework called LLM-RBDO. This method leverages the in-context learning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) and integrates them with the iterative search mechanisms of metaheuristic algorithms. The core idea is to use LLMs to generate high-quality design alternatives that satisfy reliability constraints while optimizing performance.  The framework uses prompt engineering to provide the LLM with critical information about design points. Reliability analysis is performed using LLMs and Kriging surrogate modeling to reduce computational cost. The paper demonstrates the approach's performance on a two-dimensional mathematical problem and a high-dimensional vehicle side-crash design problem using the Deepseek-V3 model. The results indicate that LLM-RBDO can find feasible solutions while maintaining a comparable convergence rate to traditional genetic algorithms (GAs).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of LLMs with RBDO is a relatively novel approach. While LLMs have been applied to other optimization problems (parametric shape optimization), this paper directly addresses the integration with reliability analysis and more complex RBDO scenarios. Using in-context learning for generative design guided by prompts tailored to RBDO is a significant departure from traditional optimization techniques.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of LLM-RBDO could be substantial if it lives up to its promises. By using LLMs to generate design solutions, it offers the possibility of more efficient exploration of the design space and potentially better solutions, especially when combined with efficient reliability analysis using surrogate models. This can significantly accelerate the design process and potentially lead to improved product performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Innovative Approach:** The integration of LLMs with RBDO is a promising direction.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** Careful design of prompts to guide LLM behavior is crucial for the success of the approach. The paper provides an example prompt that gives some insight into the procedure and considerations of the prompt engineering task.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The combination of LLMs with Kriging surrogate models could alleviate the computational burden of reliability analysis.
    *   **Case Studies:** The use of two case studies, including a high-dimensional vehicle side-crash design problem, provides some evidence of the approach's potential applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Foundation:**  The theoretical understanding of *why* and *how* LLMs are effective in this specific context remains somewhat limited. The paper primarily focuses on the practical implementation but offers little theoretical justification for the observed behavior.
    *   **Dependence on LLM Performance:**  The success of LLM-RBDO heavily relies on the capabilities of the specific LLM used (Deepseek-V3). The generalizability of the approach to other LLMs and different model sizes remains unclear.
    *   **High-Dimensional Challenges:** The second case study demonstrates that LLM-RBDO faces challenges in high-dimensional problems with multiple constraints.  The performance is comparable to SEGA but worse than traditional FORM and SEGA when the real function is used as constraint.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** The paper mentions that certain hyperparameters haven't been systematically fine-tuned.  The choice of top\_p and temperature, as well as the threshold, d, need to be justified in more detail with respect to the design task.
    *   **Scalability and Complexity:** Practical engineering problems can involve a large number of design variables, uncertainties, and constraints. The paper's demonstrations are relatively simplified compared to real-world industrial applications and thus only offer some suggestion as to the applicability of the approach.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Given the reliance on LLMs and the specific prompt engineering techniques, reproducibility may be a concern. Replicating the results exactly could be difficult without access to the same LLM and the precise implementation details.
    *   **Black-Box Nature:** The black-box nature of LLMs can make it difficult to interpret the design decisions and debug potential issues. This may limit the trust and acceptance of the approach in engineering practice.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the limitations can be addressed, LLM-RBDO could potentially transform design optimization workflows, particularly in situations where computational resources are limited or rapid design iteration is required. It has the potential to inspire further research into the integration of AI and optimization algorithms.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6**.

Here's the rationale: The paper presents an innovative idea with the potential to impact the field of design optimization. It addresses a relevant problem (RBDO) and proposes a novel solution using LLMs. The experimental results, while limited, provide some initial evidence of the approach's effectiveness. However, the paper also suffers from several limitations, including a lack of theoretical foundation, dependence on LLM performance, challenges in high-dimensional problems, limited validation, and potential reproducibility issues. More detailed experiments should have been considered, in addition to more rigorous prompt development to reduce the black-box nature of the approach. Although novel, it does not currently display sufficiently revolutionary characteristics such that it will become a staple of future research.

- **Classification**: cs.LG

### Training Large Language Models for Advanced Typosquatting Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22406v1)
- **Authors**: Jackson Welch
- **Abstract**: Typosquatting is a long-standing cyber threat that exploits human error in typing URLs to deceive users, distribute malware, and conduct phishing attacks. With the proliferation of domain names and new Top-Level Domains (TLDs), typosquatting techniques have grown more sophisticated, posing significant risks to individuals, businesses, and national cybersecurity infrastructure. Traditional detection methods primarily focus on well-known impersonation patterns, leaving gaps in identifying more complex attacks. This study introduces a novel approach leveraging large language models (LLMs) to enhance typosquatting detection. By training an LLM on character-level transformations and pattern-based heuristics rather than domain-specific data, a more adaptable and resilient detection mechanism develops. Experimental results indicate that the Phi-4 14B model outperformed other tested models when properly fine tuned achieving a 98% accuracy rate with only a few thousand training samples. This research highlights the potential of LLMs in cybersecurity applications, specifically in mitigating domain-based deception tactics, and provides insights into optimizing machine learning strategies for threat detection.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for detecting typosquatting attacks. It proposes training an LLM, specifically Phi-4 14B, on character-level transformations and pattern-based heuristics to identify deceptive domain names.  The approach involves a self-distillation process, using ChatGPT to generate synthetic training data to augment real-world examples. The authors fine-tune the Phi-4 14B model using the Unsloth library and achieve a 98% accuracy rate in distinguishing between legitimate and typosquatted domains. The paper compares the performance of different LLM architectures and parameter sizes and contrasts fine-tuning versus retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). The authors conclude that fine-tuned LLMs offer a proactive layer of defense against typosquatting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Application:** The paper tackles a relevant and persistent cybersecurity threat, typosquatting, with a practical application of LLMs.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The application of LLMs trained on character-level heuristics to typosquatting detection is a relatively novel approach. Traditional methods often rely on static lists or signature-based detections. This heuristic based LLM approach can provide a better detection rate.
    *   **Performance:** The reported 98% accuracy with the fine-tuned Phi-4 14B model is compelling.
    *   **Systematic Comparison:** The paper provides a systematic comparison of different LLM architectures (LLaMA variants, DeepSeek), highlighting the trade-offs between performance and inference speed.
    *   **Resource Efficiency:** The use of the Unsloth library and a single GPU (RTX 3090) demonstrates the feasibility of fine-tuning LLMs for this task with relatively accessible hardware.
    *   **Self-Distillation:** The use of self-distillation with ChatGPT is a smart way to augment limited real-world data and reduce dependency on large amounts of real data which can be hard to acquire.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in LLM Usage:** While the *application* to typosquatting is worthwhile, the *method* of training an LLM isn't fundamentally new. The paper leverages established techniques of fine-tuning and synthetic data generation.
    *   **Specific Model and Dataset:** The results are heavily dependent on the specific dataset used for training and validation, as well as the Phi-4 14B model. The generalizability of the approach to other datasets or models isn't fully established. The data generation with ChatGPT also inherently biases the model to the information ChatGPT has access to.
    *   **False Positives/Negatives:** The paper acknowledges false positives (misclassifying legitimate domains), but doesn't deeply analyze the types of errors and their potential impact. A real-world deployment would need to carefully address these. There could also be false negatives in this model, as typosquatters continue to change their strategies. This model is heavily dependent on the dataset used for training, and as such can be susceptible to data poisoning or adversarial attacks.
    *   **Limited Scope of Analysis:** The paper focuses on accuracy and speed, but lacks an in-depth analysis of other relevant metrics, such as precision, recall, and F1-score.
    *   **Lack of comparison to modern methods:** No comparison is made to modern non-LLM typosquatting models, making it hard to judge the LLM's performance.
    *   **Future work scope**: The scope of the future work mentions incorporating "more varied threat intelligence and real-world attack patterns" without explaining any particular methods, which are important for showing the impact of the improvement.

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper demonstrates the potential of LLMs to proactively detect typosquatting attacks, which could inspire further research and development in this area.
    *   The resource-efficient approach (Unsloth, single GPU) makes the solution more accessible to researchers and practitioners.
    *   The work could influence the development of more sophisticated cybersecurity tools and services that leverage LLMs for threat detection.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable application of LLMs to a practical cybersecurity problem. However, the novelty lies more in the application than in the underlying LLM techniques. The lack of a comparison to existing methods and limited scope of analysis also detract from the overall significance. Considering these factors, a score of 6 reflects a moderate level of novelty and a worthwhile contribution to the field, but also acknowledges the limitations of the research.

**Score: 6**
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Unveiling the Mist over 3D Vision-Language Understanding: Object-centric Evaluation with Chain-of-Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22420v1)
- **Authors**: Jiangyong Huang, Baoxiong Jia, Yan Wang, Ziyu Zhu, Xiongkun Linghu, Qing Li, Song-Chun Zhu, Siyuan Huang
- **Abstract**: Existing 3D vision-language (3D-VL) benchmarks fall short in evaluating 3D-VL models, creating a "mist" that obscures rigorous insights into model capabilities and 3D-VL tasks. This mist persists due to three key limitations. First, flawed test data, like ambiguous referential text in the grounding task, can yield incorrect and unreliable test results. Second, oversimplified metrics such as simply averaging accuracy per question answering (QA) pair, cannot reveal true model capability due to their vulnerability to language variations. Third, existing benchmarks isolate the grounding and QA tasks, disregarding the underlying coherence that QA should be based on solid grounding capabilities. To unveil the "mist", we propose Beacon3D, a benchmark for 3D-VL grounding and QA tasks, delivering a perspective shift in the evaluation of 3D-VL understanding. Beacon3D features (i) high-quality test data with precise and natural language, (ii) object-centric evaluation with multiple tests per object to ensure robustness, and (iii) a novel chain-of-analysis paradigm to address language robustness and model performance coherence across grounding and QA. Our evaluation of state-of-the-art 3D-VL models on Beacon3D reveals that (i) object-centric evaluation elicits true model performance and particularly weak generalization in QA; (ii) grounding-QA coherence remains fragile in current 3D-VL models, and (iii) incorporating large language models (LLMs) to 3D-VL models, though as a prevalent practice, hinders grounding capabilities and has yet to elevate QA capabilities. We hope Beacon3D and our comprehensive analysis could benefit the 3D-VL community towards faithful developments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BEACON3D, a new benchmark for 3D vision-language (3D-VL) grounding and question answering (QA) tasks. The authors argue that existing benchmarks suffer from flawed test data, oversimplified evaluation metrics, and a lack of coherence between grounding and QA. BEACON3D addresses these limitations by featuring high-quality test data, object-centric evaluation with multiple tests per object, and a novel chain-of-analysis paradigm using Grounding-Chains (G-Chains) and Grounding-QA-Chains (GQA-Chains). The benchmark enables a more robust and reliable assessment of 3D-VL model capabilities, revealing limitations in current models, such as weak generalization in QA, fragile grounding-QA coherence, and a potential hindrance of LLMs in grounding capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the BEACON3D benchmark and the associated analysis framework. While the individual components (3D grounding, QA, using LLMs) are not novel in themselves, the comprehensive integration of high-quality data, object-centric evaluation, and the chain-of-analysis approach is a significant step forward. The focus on grounding-QA coherence is a particularly insightful aspect that hasn't been thoroughly addressed in previous benchmarks. The analysis of LLMs hindering grounding is also a potentially valuable, albeit surprising, finding.
*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a critical issue in the 3D-VL field – the lack of reliable benchmarks that can accurately assess model capabilities. By addressing flaws in existing datasets and evaluation methods, BEACON3D has the potential to drive more faithful and robust development of 3D-VL models. The findings about LLMs are particularly impactful, as they challenge the widespread assumption that simply incorporating LLMs automatically improves performance in all aspects of 3D-VL understanding.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Detailed problem analysis:** The paper provides a thorough critique of existing benchmarks, identifying specific flaws and their impact on model evaluation.
    *   **Rigorous data curation:** The authors emphasize the careful creation of high-quality test data, addressing ambiguities and ensuring natural language descriptions.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation framework:** The object-centric evaluation and chain-of-analysis paradigm provide a more holistic assessment of model capabilities and coherence.
    *   **Insightful findings:** The analysis of state-of-the-art models reveals important limitations and challenges in 3D-VL understanding, particularly regarding grounding-QA coherence and the impact of LLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited scope:** The current scope of BEACON3D could be limited by the tasks they cover (e.g. do not consider tasks like language navigation). Expanding the benchmark to include more complex tasks and scenarios would further enhance its value.
    *   **Dependence on existing datasets:** While the authors curate high-quality subsets, the benchmark still relies on existing datasets like ScanNet. This could inherit some biases or limitations from the underlying data.
    *   **Generalizability:** There will likely be some limitations to consider in the generalizability of the experiments.

**Justification for the Score:**

BEACON3D presents a valuable contribution to the 3D-VL community by addressing critical shortcomings in existing benchmarks. The detailed analysis, rigorous data curation, and comprehensive evaluation framework have the potential to significantly improve the development and assessment of 3D-VL models. The findings regarding grounding-QA coherence and the surprising impact of LLMs provide important insights and challenge existing assumptions. While the benchmark has some limitations in scope, its potential influence on the field warrants a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CoSIL: Software Issue Localization via LLM-Driven Code Repository Graph Searching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22424v1)
- **Authors**: Zhonghao Jiang, Xiaoxue Ren, Meng Yan, Wei Jiang, Yong Li, Zhongxin Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have significantly advanced autonomous software engineering, leading to a growing number of software engineering agents that assist developers in automatic program repair. Issue localization forms the basis for accurate patch generation. However, because of limitations caused by the context window length of LLMs, existing issue localization methods face challenges in balancing concise yet effective contexts and adequately comprehensive search spaces. In this paper, we introduce CoSIL, an LLM driven, simple yet powerful function level issue localization method without training or indexing. CoSIL reduces the search space through module call graphs, iteratively searches the function call graph to obtain relevant contexts, and uses context pruning to control the search direction and manage contexts effectively. Importantly, the call graph is dynamically constructed by the LLM during search, eliminating the need for pre-parsing. Experiment results demonstrate that CoSIL achieves a Top-1 localization success rate of 43 percent and 44.6 percent on SWE bench Lite and SWE bench Verified, respectively, using Qwen2.5 Coder 32B, outperforming existing methods by 8.6 to 98.2 percent. When CoSIL is applied to guide the patch generation stage, the resolved rate further improves by 9.3 to 31.5 percent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoSIL, a novel Large Language Model (LLM)-driven approach for software issue localization. CoSIL avoids the need for pre-training or pre-built code indexes. It employs a module call graph to reduce the search space, iteratively explores the function call graph to obtain relevant contexts, and uses a context pruning mechanism to control the search direction and manage context effectively. Notably, the call graph is dynamically constructed by the LLM itself, removing the need for upfront parsing. Experimental results on SWE-bench Lite and SWE-bench Verified datasets demonstrate CoSIL's improved Top-1 localization accuracy compared to existing LLM-based methods, also positively impacting patch generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:

    *   **Dynamic Call Graph Construction:** Avoiding pre-parsing and allowing the LLM to dynamically construct the call graph during search is a key contribution, making it adaptable to constantly evolving repositories. This differs from approaches that rely on static code analysis and pre-built indices.
    *   **Module Call Graph Enhanced Reflection**: Uses reflection to identity suspicious code files, which are not explicitly mentioned in the original issue description.
    *   **Iterative Search with Pruning**: Combining iterative graph search with pruning is a smart way to balance context awareness and efficiency. The pruning mechanism helps focus the LLM's attention and prevent it from being overwhelmed by irrelevant information.
    *   **Training-Free**: Unlike methods that depend on supervised fine-tuning (SFT) of LLMs, CoSIL needs no localization data.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Improved Localization Accuracy:** The experimental results clearly show CoSIL's effectiveness in improving localization accuracy, both at the file and function level, across different LLMs. This is significant because accurate localization is fundamental to effective automated program repair.
    *   **Impact on Patch Generation:** The positive impact of CoSIL on patch generation success rate demonstrates the practical value of the proposed approach. By guiding the LLM to the correct locations, CoSIL improves the quality of generated patches.
    *   **Efficiency:** The training-free nature of CoSIL and its dynamic construction avoid reliance on expensive training processes and pre-built indices.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing LLM-based localization methods and presents a well-defined solution (CoSIL).
    *   **Well-Designed Experiments:** The experimental setup is comprehensive, using widely recognized benchmarks (SWE-bench Lite and Verified) and comparing against relevant baselines. The ablation study effectively demonstrates the contribution of each component of CoSIL.
    *   **Strong Results:** The experimental results are compelling and demonstrate the effectiveness of CoSIL in improving localization accuracy and patch generation success rate.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Variety:**  The experiments focus primarily on Python code from SWE-bench. Evaluating CoSIL on other languages and datasets would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Evaluation of Agent Utilization:** The paper finds the "function match rate" is more susceptible to the agent-driven repair stage rather than indicative of localization performance. This calls into question existing ways of evaluating localization and calls for future investigation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The CoSIL approach is likely to influence future research in LLM-based software engineering in the following ways:

    *   Encourage more research on dynamic code analysis and graph construction techniques.
    *   Promote the development of more efficient and adaptable localization methods that can handle large, evolving codebases.
    *   Inspire new approaches for integrating LLMs with program repair tools.

**Justification for Score:**

CoSIL is a substantial contribution to the field of LLM-based software engineering. Its training-free nature, dynamic call graph construction, and iterative search with pruning are novel and effective techniques for improving issue localization accuracy. The experimental results demonstrate the practical value of the approach. Its novelty and practicality, along with a fair comparison and experimentation setup, makes it a solid contribution with demonstrable potential for impact and further research. While it can be improved through evaluation on other datasets, and the discovery about function match rate warrants further investigation, these aren't sufficient to diminish the positive contribution made by this paper.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### STADE: Standard Deviation as a Pruning Metric
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22451v1)
- **Authors**: Diego Coello de Portugal Mecke, Haya Alyoussef, Ilia Koloiarov, Maximilian Stubbemann, Lars Schmidt-Thieme
- **Abstract**: Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have become very widespread and are used to solve a wide variety of tasks. To successfully handle these tasks, LLMs require longer training times and larger model sizes. This makes LLMs ideal candidates for pruning methods that reduce computational demands while maintaining performance. Previous methods require a retraining phase after pruning to maintain the original model's performance. However, state-of-the-art pruning methods, such as Wanda, prune the model without retraining, making the pruning process faster and more efficient. Building upon Wanda's work, this study provides a theoretical explanation of why the method is effective and leverages these insights to enhance the pruning process. Specifically, a theoretical analysis of the pruning problem reveals a common scenario in Machine Learning where Wanda is the optimal pruning method. Furthermore, this analysis is extended to cases where Wanda is no longer optimal, leading to the development of a new method, STADE, based on the standard deviation of the input. From a theoretical standpoint, STADE demonstrates better generality across different scenarios. Finally, extensive experiments on Llama and Open Pre-trained Transformers (OPT) models validate these theoretical findings, showing that depending on the training conditions, Wanda's optimal performance varies as predicted by the theoretical framework. These insights contribute to a more robust understanding of pruning strategies and their practical implications. Code is available at: https://github.com/Coello-dev/STADE/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "STADE: Standard Deviation as a Pruning Metric":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of pruning Large Language Models (LLMs) to reduce their computational demands. It builds upon the existing Wanda pruning method, which prunes based on the L2-norm of inputs multiplied by weight magnitudes. The authors provide a theoretical analysis explaining Wanda's effectiveness under specific conditions (unbiased inputs and no bias terms).  They then extend the analysis to cases where Wanda is suboptimal, specifically when these assumptions are violated. This leads to the development of a new pruning metric called STADE (Standard Deviation as a Pruning Metric), which considers the standard deviation of the input. STADE adaptively uses either the Wanda metric or its own, depending on whether the input is centered (zero mean).  The paper presents experimental results on Llama and OPT models, showing that STADE can outperform Wanda, particularly in layers where inputs are not centered. The paper also investigates applying different pruning metrics to different layers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides a good theoretical contribution. The theoretical analysis to justify why a certain pruning method works better than another is an important element. The derivation of STADE, based on a detailed analysis of the limitations of Wanda, adds incremental value to the field. Specifically, using a standard deviation-based measure (STADE) based on centering. The use of different pruning metrics for different layers represents a novel approach and motivates future research.

*   **Significance:** Reducing the size and computational cost of LLMs is a critical problem. While many pruning techniques exist, theoretical justifications that explain *why* certain methods are effective are important. The authors provide valuable insights into pruning effectiveness based on statistical input properties, which can inform future pruning strategies. Showing how different layers benefit from different metrics has practical implications for model compression. The experimental results validate the theoretical findings. However, there are some flaws.
    *   The gains of STADE over Wanda, while present, seem relatively incremental, especially in the zero-shot evaluation (Table 4). The improvements are not of great scale. This does not necessarily mean that the method is not good, but that it might not be of great influence within the field.

    *   The paper doesn't deeply explore the computational overhead of calculating the standard deviation. Although STADE prunes without retraining, calculating per-layer or even per-neuron standard deviations may add to the complexity, depending on how these statistics are computed. This is a good element for improvement for future research.

    *   The ablation study on STADE with/without bias is somewhat limited. A more granular analysis of specific layer types (beyond just whether they receive normalized input) and a wider range of sparsity levels would strengthen this section.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and the theoretical derivations are explained well. The experimental setup is clearly defined. The tables are well-organized and provide a clear comparison of the different methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a reasonable theoretical justification of the underlying workings of the Wanda method, based on some standard ML concepts. The authors then leverage these theoretical understandings to propose a useful pruning metric and provide experimental justification of this metric. While the improvements aren't drastic and the computational considerations could be discussed more deeply, the paper advances the state-of-the-art. However, the results are incremental, and for this reason a very high score is not deserved.

- **Score**: 7/10

### WorkTeam: Constructing Workflows from Natural Language with Multi-Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22473v1)
- **Authors**: Hanchao Liu, Rongjun Li, Weimin Xiong, Ziyu Zhou, Wei Peng
- **Abstract**: Workflows play a crucial role in enhancing enterprise efficiency by orchestrating complex processes with multiple tools or components. However, hand-crafted workflow construction requires expert knowledge, presenting significant technical barriers. Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have improved the generation of workflows from natural language instructions (aka NL2Workflow), yet existing single LLM agent-based methods face performance degradation on complex tasks due to the need for specialized knowledge and the strain of task-switching. To tackle these challenges, we propose WorkTeam, a multi-agent NL2Workflow framework comprising a supervisor, orchestrator, and filler agent, each with distinct roles that collaboratively enhance the conversion process. As there are currently no publicly available NL2Workflow benchmarks, we also introduce the HW-NL2Workflow dataset, which includes 3,695 real-world business samples for training and evaluation. Experimental results show that our approach significantly increases the success rate of workflow construction, providing a novel and effective solution for enterprise NL2Workflow services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "WorkTeam," a multi-agent framework designed to enhance the construction of workflows from natural language instructions (NL2Workflow). Recognizing the limitations of single LLM-based approaches when dealing with complex, real-world instructions, WorkTeam employs three agents: a supervisor, an orchestrator, and a filler. Each agent has a distinct role: the supervisor manages task planning and result reflection; the orchestrator selects and arranges appropriate components; and the filler populates component parameters. The paper also contributes a new dataset, HW-NL2Workflow, comprising 3,695 real-world enterprise workflow examples. Experimental results demonstrate that WorkTeam significantly improves workflow construction accuracy compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a multi-agent approach for NL2Workflow is a significant step forward. Breaking down the complex workflow generation process into specialized agent roles is a logical and innovative approach to tackle the limitations of single-agent LLM systems, which often struggle with task-switching and specialized knowledge requirements. This decomposition is inspired by collaborative software development practices, making it a reasonable and potentially fruitful analogy.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in the improved performance and the introduction of a real-world dataset. Current research on NL2Workflow primarily uses general or synthetic data. The HW-NL2Workflow dataset is therefore highly valuable for benchmarking and training models that are more applicable to enterprise settings. The performance increase reported by WorkTeam, particularly the improvement in exact match rate (EMR), suggests a significant advancement in the field. The paper provides compelling evidence that the multi-agent approach can lead to substantial improvements in accuracy and reliability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Roles:**  The division of labor among the supervisor, orchestrator, and filler agents is well-articulated and justified. This specialization allows each agent to focus on a specific aspect of the workflow generation process, leading to more accurate and consistent results.
    *   **Real-World Dataset:** The HW-NL2Workflow dataset addresses a critical gap in the field by providing a benchmark based on real-world enterprise workflows.
    *   **Significant Performance Gains:** The experimental results demonstrate a substantial improvement over existing single-agent LLM-based methods, highlighting the effectiveness of the multi-agent approach.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies are conducted, which reveal the contribution of each agent.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Implementation Details:** While the paper outlines the framework and agent roles, it lacks detailed information on the specific LLM prompts and training data used for each agent. More transparency in this area would allow for better reproducibility and facilitate further research. The prompts should be added as appendix material, but they do appear there.
    *   **Dependency on Large Models:** The reliance on large language models (LLMs) for all agents raises concerns about computational cost and scalability, though the use of different LLMs (Qwen and LLaMA3) demonstrates a good understanding of current SOTA strategies. The practical deployment of WorkTeam in resource-constrained environments may pose challenges.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** Although the paper compares against baseline methods, a more thorough comparison with other multi-agent systems, even those designed for different tasks, would further strengthen the paper's contribution by clarifying WorkTeam's unique advantages.
    *   **Limited External Dataset Evaluation:** The paper could be strengthened by evaluating the framework on existing benchmark NL2Workflow datasets if they were available, despite the authors' stated focus on real-world application.

*   **Potential Influence:** WorkTeam provides a strong foundation for future research in NL2Workflow, which can influence the development of more robust and reliable automation solutions for enterprise settings. The idea of a collaborative multi-agent system can serve as a blueprint for tackling other complex NLP tasks. The public availability of the HW-NL2Workflow dataset can help spur further advancements in the field.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective multi-agent framework for the NL2Workflow problem. The combination of a well-designed system architecture, a significant performance boost, and a new, valuable real-world dataset warrants a high score. While there are some areas where the paper could be strengthened, the core contribution is substantial. The weaknesses are primarily related to lack of detail rather than fundamental flaws.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL

### Probabilistic Uncertain Reward Model: A Natural Generalization of Bradley-Terry Reward Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22480v1)
- **Authors**: Wangtao Sun, Xiang Cheng, Xing Yu, Haotian Xu, Zhao Yang, Shizhu He, Jun Zhao, Kang Liu
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) has emerged as a critical technique for training large language models. However, reward hacking-a phenomenon where models exploit flaws in the reward model-remains a significant barrier to achieving robust and scalable intelligence through long-term training. Existing studies have proposed uncertain reward model to address reward hacking, however, they often lack systematic or theoretical foundations, failing to model the uncertainty intrinsically emerging from preference data. In this paper, we propose the Probabilistic Uncertain Reward Model (PURM), a natural generalization of the classical Bradley-Terry reward model. PURM learns reward distributions directly from preference data and quantifies per-sample uncertainty via the average overlap area between reward distributions. To mitigate reward hacking, we further introduce an uncertainty-aware penalty into Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), which leverages the learned uncertainty to dynamically balance reward optimization and exploration. We propose a lightweight and easy-to-use implementation of PURM. Experiments demonstrate that PURM significantly delays the onset of reward hacking while improving final reward performance, outperforming baseline methods in both stability and effectiveness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Probabilistic Uncertain Reward Model (PURM) as a natural generalization of the Bradley-Terry reward model used in Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF).  PURM aims to address the problem of reward hacking, where models exploit flaws in reward signals. It learns reward distributions from preference data, quantifies uncertainty using the Bhattacharyya coefficient, and incorporates an uncertainty-aware penalty into Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) to balance reward optimization and exploration.  The authors demonstrate that PURM delays reward hacking, improves reward performance, and outperforms baselines in stability and effectiveness with minimal code modifications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a significant problem:** Reward hacking is a well-known and crucial issue in RLHF, hindering the development of robust and scalable AI systems.
*   **Principled approach:**  The paper offers a theoretically grounded approach by generalizing the Bradley-Terry model to a probabilistic framework and employing the Bhattacharyya coefficient for uncertainty quantification. This contrasts with some existing uncertainty-aware methods that rely on ad-hoc balancing strategies.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results show clear improvements in delaying reward hacking and achieving higher final rewards compared to baselines. The authors also demonstrate PURM's ability to detect aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty through controlled experiments.
*   **Lightweight Implementation:** The simplicity of implementing PURM (minimal code changes) enhances its practicality and adoption potential.
*   **Demonstrates improvements in real-world examples**: Figure 6 & 7 provides compelling evidence of the benefits of PURM over BTRM by showing how it can avoid reward hacking that leads to harmful responses in real-world scenarios.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** The paper builds on existing work in uncertain reward modeling and reinforcement learning. While the specific combination of ideas and the natural generalization approach are novel, the individual components are not entirely new.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the implementation is lightweight, the use of Monte Carlo sampling to approximate the integral in Eq. 3 could potentially increase computational cost compared to deterministic Bradley-Terry reward models, especially for large-scale models and datasets. This aspect should have been discussed more explicitly.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of PURM depends on the choice of the uncertainty penalty weight (λ). While the appendix briefly discusses its effect, a more thorough analysis of hyperparameter sensitivity and guidelines for selecting appropriate values would enhance the paper's practical value.
*   **Limited Scope:** The experiments focus primarily on language-based tasks. Evaluating PURM's effectiveness in other domains, such as robotics or game playing, would further strengthen its generalizability.
*   **Baselines Comparison:** While the baselines are well-chosen, the paper could benefit from comparing against more recent and advanced reward modeling techniques, especially in specific sub-areas. This would put the performance of PURM in a better context.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a solid contribution by providing a principled and effective method for mitigating reward hacking in RLHF. The generalization of the Bradley-Terry model to a probabilistic framework and the use of the Bhattacharyya coefficient for uncertainty quantification provide a novel and theoretically sound approach. The lightweight implementation and empirical validation demonstrate the practical value of PURM. The paper provides an incremental improvement over existing techniques in URM, demonstrating strong empirical results.

**Rationale for Score:**

While PURM offers a valuable and practical solution to a significant problem in RLHF, its novelty is incremental, rather than revolutionary. The paper combines existing concepts in a novel way and demonstrates strong empirical results, but it lacks a truly transformative element that would significantly shift the field's direction. The lack of in-depth hyperparameter analysis and limited scope of the experiments also contribute to the overall score. Therefore, a score of 7 is warranted, recognizing the paper's strong contributions while acknowledging its limitations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SPDNet: Seasonal-Periodic Decomposition Network for Advanced Residential Demand Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22485v1)
- **Authors**: Reza Nematirad, Anil Pahwa, Balasubramaniam Natarajan
- **Abstract**: Residential electricity demand forecasting is critical for efficient energy management and grid stability. Accurate predictions enable utility companies to optimize planning and operations. However, real-world residential electricity demand data often exhibit intricate temporal variability, including multiple seasonalities, periodicities, and abrupt fluctuations, which pose significant challenges for forecasting models. Previous models that rely on statistical methods, recurrent, convolutional neural networks, and transformers often struggle to capture these intricate temporal dynamics. To address these challenges, we propose the Seasonal-Periodic Decomposition Network (SPDNet), a novel deep learning framework consisting of two main modules. The first is the Seasonal-Trend Decomposition Module (STDM), which decomposes the input data into trend, seasonal, and residual components. The second is the Periodical Decomposition Module (PDM), which employs the Fast Fourier Transform to identify the dominant periods. For each dominant period, 1D input data is reshaped into a 2D tensor, where rows represent periods and columns correspond to frequencies. The 2D representations are then processed through three submodules: a 1D convolution to capture sharp fluctuations, a transformer-based encoder to model global patterns, and a 2D convolution to capture interactions between periods. Extensive experiments conducted on real-world residential electricity load data demonstrate that SPDNet outperforms traditional and advanced models in both forecasting accuracy and computational efficiency. The code is available in this repository: https://github.com/Tims2D/SPDNet.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided research paper "SPDNet: Seasonal-Periodic Decomposition Network for Advanced Residential Demand Forecasting" and will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and a justified novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SPDNet, a novel deep learning framework designed to improve the accuracy of residential electricity demand forecasting.  Residential load forecasting is difficult due to temporal variations in electricity usage.  The core idea involves decomposing the time series data into seasonal, trend, and residual components, and then analyzing periodicities in the data through Fourier transform analysis. The extracted periodic components are then processed in parallel through 1D convolutional, transformer-based, and 2D convolutional modules to capture short-term fluctuations, global patterns, and inter-period dependencies respectively. Finally, the outputs of these modules are combined to produce a final forecast. The authors demonstrate that SPDNet outperforms existing models (RNNs, LSTMs, Transformers, etc.) in terms of forecasting accuracy and computational efficiency on real-world residential electricity load data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Problem Relevance:** Residential load forecasting is a crucial problem with increasing importance in smart grids and demand-side management. Accurate forecasts enable better grid management and integration of distributed energy resources.
*   **Sound Methodology:** The proposed SPDNet architecture is well-reasoned.  The decomposition of time series data is a standard and effective approach in time series analysis and forecasting. The use of FFT to identify periodicities is appropriate. Parallel processing of 2D representations using convolution and transformers is an interesting design choice to capture varying temporal dependencies.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The authors compare SPDNet against a broad range of baseline models, including traditional and state-of-the-art deep learning techniques. The evaluation is conducted on real-world datasets, and the results consistently demonstrate superior performance for SPDNet. The inclusion of both MSE and MAE as evaluation metrics is standard and allows for a comprehensive assessment of the forecasting performance. The examination of training times per epoch further supports the claim of improved computational efficiency.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written, with clear explanations of the proposed methodology and experimental results. The figures and tables are informative and support the claims made in the text.
*   **Code Availability:**  The availability of the code is a positive aspect. It will enable other researchers to reproduce the results and further explore the SPDNet architecture.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:**  While the overall architecture is novel, the individual components (seasonal decomposition, FFT, convolution, transformers) are well-established techniques. The novelty lies primarily in their specific combination and application to residential load forecasting.
*   **Lack of In-depth Ablation Study:** While the results show improvement compared to many standard baselines, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth ablation study to understand the contribution of each module within SPDNet (e.g., removing the STDM or PDM to assess their individual impact).  This would help to isolate the factors driving the performance gains. The current results mainly show performance improvements by SPDNet as a single unit.
*   **Hyperparameter Optimization:** The hyperparameters were optimized with residential load 1, and these settings were applied to other datasets. The different loads are likely to exhibit different temporal usage patterns, and optimizing the hyperparameters for each load could improve results further.
*   **Limited Discussion on Failure Cases:**  While the paper focuses on the successes of SPDNet, it would be valuable to discuss potential failure cases or scenarios where the model might not perform as well. This would provide a more balanced and realistic assessment of the model's capabilities. For example, discussion on the type of usage patterns it performs well and how it handles extreme events.
* **No Discussion of Advantages over Times2D:** In the introduction, the paper mentions that SPDNet is a modification of Times2D. A section should be included that explicitly specifies the advantages of SPDNet over Times2D.

*Significance:*

The paper contributes to the growing body of research on residential load forecasting by introducing a novel deep learning architecture that combines several established techniques in a unique and effective way. The demonstrated improvements in forecasting accuracy and computational efficiency are significant and could have practical implications for utility companies and smart grid operators. The open-source code will also facilitate further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

*   **Rationale:** The paper presents a technically sound methodology for a relevant and important problem. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of SPDNet compared to several baseline models.  The main strength lies in the effective integration of the seasonal, trend, and periodicity analysis, and using this information in deep-learning model. However, the novelty of the individual components is limited, and the lack of in-depth ablation studies and discussion of failure cases prevents a higher score. While the performance improvements are noteworthy, the long-term impact will depend on the model's ability to generalize to other datasets and adapt to changing consumption patterns. Further optimization would likely increase performance even more.
*   **Why not higher?** A score of 8 or above would require a more significant breakthrough in methodology or a more comprehensive analysis of the model's limitations. More rigorous testing of each component is required.
*   **Why not lower?** A score below 7 would not adequately recognize the technical quality of the work, the comprehensive experimental evaluation, and the potential practical impact of SPDNet in residential load forecasting.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scenario Dreamer: Vectorized Latent Diffusion for Generating Driving Simulation Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22496v1)
- **Authors**: Luke Rowe, Roger Girgis, Anthony Gosselin, Liam Paull, Christopher Pal, Felix Heide
- **Abstract**: We introduce Scenario Dreamer, a fully data-driven generative simulator for autonomous vehicle planning that generates both the initial traffic scene - comprising a lane graph and agent bounding boxes - and closed-loop agent behaviours. Existing methods for generating driving simulation environments encode the initial traffic scene as a rasterized image and, as such, require parameter-heavy networks that perform unnecessary computation due to many empty pixels in the rasterized scene. Moreover, we find that existing methods that employ rule-based agent behaviours lack diversity and realism. Scenario Dreamer instead employs a novel vectorized latent diffusion model for initial scene generation that directly operates on the vectorized scene elements and an autoregressive Transformer for data-driven agent behaviour simulation. Scenario Dreamer additionally supports scene extrapolation via diffusion inpainting, enabling the generation of unbounded simulation environments. Extensive experiments show that Scenario Dreamer outperforms existing generative simulators in realism and efficiency: the vectorized scene-generation base model achieves superior generation quality with around 2x fewer parameters, 6x lower generation latency, and 10x fewer GPU training hours compared to the strongest baseline. We confirm its practical utility by showing that reinforcement learning planning agents are more challenged in Scenario Dreamer environments than traditional non-generative simulation environments, especially on long and adversarial driving environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Scenario Dreamer: Vectorized Latent Diffusion for Generating Driving Simulation Environments" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Scenario Dreamer, a novel data-driven generative simulator for autonomous vehicle (AV) planning. Unlike existing methods that rely on rasterized images of the driving scene, Scenario Dreamer employs a vectorized latent diffusion model (VLDM) for generating initial traffic scenes (lane graphs and agent bounding boxes). It then uses a return-conditioned autoregressive Transformer to simulate closed-loop agent behaviors.  The system supports scene extrapolation through diffusion inpainting, enabling the creation of unbounded simulation environments. The authors demonstrate superior performance in realism and efficiency compared to existing generative simulators, with their VLDM achieving higher generation quality, reduced latency, and lower training costs.  Finally, they show that reinforcement learning planning agents are more challenged within Scenario Dreamer environments, highlighting its practical utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel elements:

    *   **Vectorized Latent Diffusion Model (VLDM):** This is the most significant contribution. Moving away from rasterized BEV images to a vectorized representation directly addresses the computational inefficiencies of handling large empty spaces. The explicit modeling of lane graph connectivity through pairwise relationships is also a notable improvement.
    *   **Data-Driven Behavior Simulation with Transformer:** The adoption of a Transformer-based behavior model, conditioned on returns, provides a more realistic and controllable alternative to rule-based agent behaviors. The ability to generate adversarial driving scenarios by tilting the return model is a valuable feature.
    *   **Scene Extrapolation via Diffusion Inpainting:** While diffusion inpainting is not entirely novel, its application to generating unbounded driving scenes in a vectorized format is a useful extension.
*   **Significance:**
    *   **Addressing Simulator Limitations:** The paper directly addresses the limitations of current driving simulators that are constrained by the size and diversity of pre-recorded driving logs. The generative approach offers a scalable alternative for training and evaluating AVs.
    *   **Improved Efficiency:** The demonstrated improvements in generation latency, parameter count, and training time are significant. These efficiencies make the simulator more accessible and practical for real-world applications.
    *   **Challenging RL Planners:** The paper provides evidence that RL planners are more challenged in Scenario Dreamer environments, indicating that it generates more complex and realistic scenarios than existing non-generative simulators.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a strong motivation for the need for generative driving simulators.
    *   The technical details of the VLDM and Transformer-based behavior model are well-explained.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, comparing Scenario Dreamer against competitive baselines and demonstrating its advantages.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different design choices.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper emphasizes the data-driven nature of the approach, it would be beneficial to have more details on the specific training data used and how it might influence the realism and diversity of the generated scenarios. Are the training data representative of all possible driving conditions and environments?
    *   It is interesting that the trained agents are more challenged in Scenario Dreamer, but more information about the specific types of challenges encountered by the RL agents is needed. For instance, are agents challenged by complex road geometry, diverse traffic conditions, or both?
    *   Future work should investigate how well a planner trained on the generated environments transfers to the real world.
*   **Potential Influence:** Scenario Dreamer has the potential to influence the development of more scalable, efficient, and realistic driving simulators. The vectorized latent diffusion model could become a standard approach for generating initial traffic scenes. The framework could also stimulate further research on data-driven behavior simulation and the generation of adversarial driving scenarios.
    *   There are statements claiming that their agent model is better than those that rely on imitation learning or just RL. This is a bold claim that needs to be supported or more nuanced.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**. The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of driving simulation. The novelty of the vectorized latent diffusion model, the efficiency improvements, and the demonstrated ability to generate challenging scenarios for RL planners justify this score. The paper's strong experimental validation and clear presentation further strengthen its value. The weaknesses discussed above regarding the transferability to the real world and influence of training data prevent a higher score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### Masked Self-Supervised Pre-Training for Text Recognition Transformers on Large-Scale Datasets
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22513v1)
- **Authors**: Martin Kišš, Michal Hradiš
- **Abstract**: Self-supervised learning has emerged as a powerful approach for leveraging large-scale unlabeled data to improve model performance in various domains. In this paper, we explore masked self-supervised pre-training for text recognition transformers. Specifically, we propose two modifications to the pre-training phase: progressively increasing the masking probability, and modifying the loss function to incorporate both masked and non-masked patches. We conduct extensive experiments using a dataset of 50M unlabeled text lines for pre-training and four differently sized annotated datasets for fine-tuning. Furthermore, we compare our pre-trained models against those trained with transfer learning, demonstrating the effectiveness of the self-supervised pre-training. In particular, pre-training consistently improves the character error rate of models, in some cases up to 30 % relatively. It is also on par with transfer learning but without relying on extra annotated text lines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores masked self-supervised pre-training for text recognition using Transformer models.  The authors propose two modifications to the standard pre-training approach: (1) progressively increasing the masking probability during pre-training, and (2) modifying the loss function to consider both masked and non-masked patches during training. They conduct experiments using a large dataset of 50 million unlabeled text lines for pre-training, followed by fine-tuning on four differently sized annotated datasets. They compare their pre-trained models with those trained using transfer learning from existing OCR models. The results show consistent improvements in character error rate (CER) with pre-training, and demonstrate performance on par with transfer learning, but without needing extra annotated text lines beyond the target task.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of self-supervised learning for text recognition is not entirely new, with existing work such as MaskOCR, CCD and others using masked label prediction and contrastive learning. The specific contributions of this paper lie in the proposed modifications to the pre-training procedure: progressively increasing the masking probability and incorporating non-masked patches into the loss. While seemingly incremental, these modifications can potentially lead to substantial improvements. The masking strategy is similar to curriculum learning in sequence-to-sequence models, although it's applied in the pre-training stage here. The use of non-masked patches can be considered an efficient improvement of the pre-training, as the model utilizes more information from the input data. The claim of novelty hinges on the specific combination and evaluation of these modifications within the context of text recognition Transformers, along with the use of a particularly large unlabeled dataset.

**Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its potential to reduce the reliance on large, annotated datasets for training text recognition models. Since creating large HTR datasets is costly, self-supervised pre-training is an important direction. By effectively leveraging unlabeled data, the proposed approach could enable better performance with limited labeled resources, making text recognition more accessible in various domains. The experimental results seem promising, indicating consistent improvements in CER across different datasets and model sizes. The comparison with transfer learning is also important, as it shows the potential for self-supervised pre-training to be competitive with a standard approach.  However, the observed improvements are relative to training from scratch, and it's crucial to evaluate against more recent self-supervised approaches to fully demonstrate the contribution. State-of-the-art models are only compared for the final evaluation, this should also be compared when analysing the pre-trained models.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation, including ablation studies of the proposed modifications and comparisons with transfer learning and state-of-the-art models.
*   **Large-Scale Pre-training:** Utilizing a large unlabeled dataset (50M text lines) allows for robust pre-training and provides meaningful results.
*   **Clear and Concise Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, making the contributions clear.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** The proposed modifications, while effective, are relatively incremental and build on existing ideas in self-supervised learning and curriculum learning.
*   **Limited Comparison to SOTA Self-Supervised Methods:** The comparison with transfer learning is valuable, but it would strengthen the paper to compare it against other self-supervised learning techniques for text recognition directly.
*   **Decoder size limitations:** The authors should also include models with larger decoders to prove their self-supervised learning strategy works on better models.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the limitations, a score of 7 is justified. The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by demonstrating the effectiveness of masked self-supervised pre-training for text recognition, with a thorough evaluation of proposed modifications. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and a more extensive comparison with existing self-supervised methods and larger decoders would further solidify the claims. The work has the potential to influence future research in text recognition and reduce the reliance on labeled data, but the extent of its impact remains to be seen.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploiting Mixture-of-Experts Redundancy Unlocks Multimodal Generative Abilities
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22517v1)
- **Authors**: Raman Dutt, Harleen Hanspal, Guoxuan Xia, Petru-Daniel Tudosiu, Alexander Black, Yongxin Yang, Steven McDonagh, Sarah Parisot
